A person born with the Moon in the sign of **Leo** <term>Siṃha: The fifth sign of the zodiac, represented by the Lion</term> is forgiving, modest, and always fond of meat and wine <note>A traditional astrological indicator of a "Rajasic" or passionate/royal temperament</note>. He is a traveler of many lands, has a fear of the cold, and possesses beautiful friends. He is humble yet quick to anger, beloved by his mother and the public, prone to certain vices, and becomes famous throughout the world. || 8-9 ||

> A seeker of luxury, a promoter of happiness for good people, filled with auspicious signs.
> A donor, skillful, a wise poet, and devoted to the path of the Vedas. || 10 ||
> Beloved of all people, devoted to the arts of dance and music.
> A traveler, suffering because of women—such is the man born in the sign of Virgo. || 11 ||

A person born with the Moon in the sign of **Virgo** <term>Kanyā: The sixth sign of the zodiac, represented by the Maiden</term> is fond of luxury, brings joy to virtuous people, and is endowed with beautiful physical traits. He is a generous giver, capable, wise like an elder poet, and follows the path of the Vedas <note>Ancient sacred scriptures of India</note>. He is liked by everyone, takes pleasure in dance and celestial music <note>original: "gāndharva," referring to the arts of the Gandharvas</note>, is fond of living abroad, and experiences sorrow on account of women. || 10-11 ||

> Angry within his own place, sorrowful, a clever speaker, and compassionate.

> Skillful in trade, a worshipper of the gods, and affectionate toward friends.
> A traveler, dear to his well-wishers—such is the man born in the sign of Libra. || 13 ||

A person born with the Moon in the sign of **Libra** <term>Tulā: The seventh sign of the zodiac, represented by the Scales</term> is prone to anger at home and feelings of sorrow, yet is a clever conversationalist. He is compassionate, has darting or restless eyes, is prosperous, and displays great strength or influence within his household. He is expert in business and trade, a devotee of the gods, loves his friends, travels frequently, and is highly cherished by his companions. || 12-13 ||

> A traveler from childhood, harsh-souled, brave, and having reddish-brown eyes.
> Devoted to the wives of others, proud, and cruel toward his own kin and others. || 14 ||
> Attaining wealth through daring acts, having a wicked mind even toward his mother.
> A cunning thief and an initiator of strife—such is the man born in Scorpio. || 15 ||

A person born with the Moon in the sign of **Scorpio** <term>Vṛścika: The eighth sign of the zodiac, represented by the Scorpion</term> is a traveler in foreign lands during their childhood. They possess a cruel nature, are powerful and courageous, and have yellow eyes. Such a person is attracted to the wives of others, is full of pride, and acts heartlessly toward their own kin. They acquire wealth <note>original: "Lakshmi," the goddess of wealth</note> through daring or risky ventures. They may harbor ill intentions even toward their mother, and are characterized as deceitful, a thief, and an initiator of various arts. || 14-15 ||

> Brave, endowed with an even mind, pure in spirit <note>original: "Sāttviko," referring to the quality of goodness and harmony</note>, and a delighter of people. 
> Accomplished in the science of crafts, wealthy, and possessing a beautiful wife. || 16 ||
> Full of pride, endowed with virtuous character, and a speaker of elegant words. 
> Radiant, possessing a stout body, the one born in Sagittarius is the ender of the lineage <note>original: "kulāntakaḥ," which can mean one who brings an end to the family line or one who stands out as the ultimate member of it</note>. || 17 ||

A person born with the Moon in the sign of **Sagittarius** <term>Dhanu: The ninth sign of the zodiac, represented by the Archer</term> is valiant, possesses a balanced intellect, and is of a pure (Sattvic) temperament. He brings happiness to others, is knowledgeable in craftsmanship and the arts, and is endowed with wealth. He is captivating, a speaker of beautiful words, an orator, majestic in presence, has a large or stout physique, and is the one who brings his lineage to its conclusion. || 16-17 ||

> Distinguished in the family, under the influence of women, a scholar, and a family man. 
> A knower of songs, full of desire, secretive, blessed with many sons, and devoted to his mother. || 18 ||
> Wealthy, generous, having good servants, compassionate, and having many kinsmen. 
> One who ponders over his own happiness—such a man is born in Capricorn. || 19 ||

A person born in the sign of **Capricorn** <term>Makara: The tenth sign of the zodiac, represented by a sea-creature or goat-fish</term> is the most excellent in his family, yet remains under the influence of women. He is a scholar, devoted to his family, clever in the art of music, and full of longing. He possesses "hidden" sons <note>The Hindi text "gupta putro" suggests either sons from secret or secondary unions, or perhaps sons whose potential is not immediately apparent</note>, is very dear to his mother, and is wealthy and charitable. He is handsome, possesses good servants, is compassionate, has many brothers, and is always concerned with his own comfort and happiness. || 18-19 ||

> A giver, yet lazy; grateful, and a master of elephants, horses, and wealth. 
> Possessing an auspicious gaze, always gentle, and making efforts in honor and education. || 20 ||
> Rich in merit, yet devoid of affection; wealthy and an enjoyer of life through his own power. 
> Created with a waist like a tiger <note>original: "shāldūrakukṣi," referring to a lean, athletic midsection</note>—such is the man born in Aquarius. || 21 ||

A person born under the sign of **Aquarius** <term>Kumbha: The eleventh sign of the zodiac, represented by the Water-Bearer</term> is charitable, lazy, and grateful. They are the master of horses, elephants, and great wealth. They possess a favorable gaze, a perpetually beautiful temperament, and are full of pride. They are diligent in their pursuit of learning and are meritorious. Though they may sometimes be lacking in affection, they are wealthy and enjoy life’s pleasures according to their capacity. They possess a midsection resembling that of a *Shalura* bird <note>original: "shālūra," which can refer to a frog; this indicates a specific physical description of the abdomen</note> and are entirely fearless. || 20-21 ||

> He is grave in his actions, a hero, clever, an eloquent speaker, and the best of men. 
> He is prone to anger, frugal, wise, the most distinguished of his lineage, and dear to his family. || 22 ||
> He is an eternal servant, swift in his desires, skilled in the Gandharva arts <note>original: "gāndharva-kuśalaḥ," referring to proficiency in music, dance, and the fine arts</note>, and auspicious. 
> One born in the sign of Pisces is also characterized by a deep affection for his kinsmen. || 23 ||

A person born in the sign of **Pisces** <term>Mīna: The twelfth sign of the zodiac, represented by the Fish</term> is serious in his endeavors, brave, and highly proficient. He speaks with a sweet voice and is considered superior among men. He is irritable and thrifty, yet possesses great knowledge. He is the pride of his family and loves his kin. He is always ready to be of service, moves with a quick gait, is an expert in singing, possesses a virtuous nature, and is well-loved by his relatives. || 22-23 ||

### A Brief Summary of the Results of the Signs from Aries Onward

> In Aries, one is humble; in Taurus, proud; and in Gemini, one possesses a clever intellect.
> One is cruel in Cancer; patient in Leo; and in Virgo, one is full of great illusions. || 1 ||
> In Libra, one has a feminine nature; in Scorpio, one is proud; and in Sagittarius, a man has sinful intentions.
> One is outspoken in Capricorn; clever in Aquarius; and in Pisces, one possesses a steady mind. || 2 ||

He whose birth occurs in **Aries** is humble <note>original: "dina," which can mean poor, wretched, or humble</note>; in **Taurus**, he is proud; in **Gemini**, he is endowed with a sharp wit; in **Cancer**, he is cruel; in **Leo**, he is patient; in **Virgo**, he is highly deceptive; in **Libra**, he has a nature similar to women; in **Scorpio**, he is full of pride; in **Sagittarius**, he is prone to sin; in **Capricorn**, he is outspoken <note>original: "mukhar," meaning talkative or one who leads with his words</note>; in **Aquarius**, he is clever; and in **Pisces**, he possesses a firm and steady intellect. || 1-2 ||

# Now, the Results of Birth in the Ascendants Starting with Aries

->One born in the **Aries** Ascendant is fierce, proud, wise, and auspicious.<-
->He is prone to anger, a subduer of people, courageous, and affectionate toward others. || 1 ||<-

A person born in the **Aries** <term>Mesha: The first sign of the zodiac, represented by the Ram</term> Ascendant is harsh, full of self-respect, a scholar, irritable, a conqueror of men <note>original: "janahantā," literally 'killer of people,' which in an astrological context suggests a fierce temperament or one who overcomes opponents</note>, powerful, and shows great love toward other people. || 1 ||

->A child born in the **Taurus** Ascendant is a devotee of his teachers and speaks pleasantly.<-
->He is virtuous, accomplished, wealthy, greedy, a hero, and beloved by everyone. || 2 ||<-

A child born in the **Taurus** <term>Vṛṣa: The second sign of the zodiac, represented by the Bull</term> Ascendant is a devotee of his Guru (teacher), speaks sweet words, is talented, a scholar, possesses a desire for wealth, is a brave warrior, and is dear to all people. || 2 ||

->One born at the rising of **Gemini** is proud and affectionate toward his own kin.<-
->He is self-sacrificing, an enjoyer of pleasures, wealthy, lustful, a procrastinator, and a crusher of enemies. || 3 ||<-

One born in the **Gemini** <term>Mithuna: The third sign of the zodiac, represented by the Twins</term> Ascendant is proud, seeks the welfare of his relatives, is charitable, enjoys life’s comforts, is wealthy, possesses strong desires, is a procrastinator <term>Dīrghasūtrī: Literally "one of long threads," referring to someone who takes a long time to complete tasks or delays action</term>, and is a destroyer of enemies. || 3 ||

->One born in the **Cancer** Ascendant is an enjoyer of pleasures, righteous, and popular.<-
->He enjoys sweet food and drink, is fortunate, and is dear to his kinsmen. || 4 ||<-

One born in the **Cancer** <term>Karka: The fourth sign of the zodiac, represented by the Crab</term> Ascendant is an enjoyer of life, a follower of Dharma (righteousness), popular among all, a lover of sweet foods and beverages, possesses great prosperity, and is dear to his own family. || 4 ||

->One born at the rising of the **Leo** Ascendant is an enjoyer of pleasures and a crusher of enemies.<-
->He has a small belly, few sons, is enthusiastic, and shows valor in battle. || 5 ||<-

One born in the **Leo** <term>Siṃha: The fifth sign of the zodiac, represented by the Lion</term> Ascendant is an enjoyer of comforts, a destroyer of his foes, possesses a small midsection, has few children, is full of zeal, and is courageous on the battlefield. || 5 ||

->A child born in the **Virgo** Ascendant is an expert in various scriptures.<-
->He is endowed with the qualities of good fortune, is handsome, and is fond of carnal pleasures. || 6 ||<-

A child born in the **Virgo** <term>Kanyā: The sixth sign of the zodiac, represented by the Maiden</term> Ascendant is highly proficient in various branches of knowledge <term>Śāstra-viśārada: One who has mastered the traditional sciences or sacred texts</term>. He is gifted with good fortune and noble virtues, is physically beautiful, and is fond of sensual enjoyment. || 6 ||

A child born in the **Pisces** <term>Meena Lagna: The twelfth sign of the zodiac, represented by two fish</term> Ascendant is filled with gems and gold, possesses few worldly desires, is extremely affectionate, and is a deep thinker who contemplates matters over a long period of time. || 12 ||

### Now, the Fruits of the Sub-Divisions of the Birth Sign

<note>The text now moves into "Navamsha" results. In Vedic astrology, each zodiac sign is divided into nine equal parts (sub-divisions) called Navamshas. Each part has a distinct influence on the individual's destiny.</note>

If one is born in the **first sub-division** <term>Navamsha: A 3° 20' arc of a zodiac sign</term> of the birth sign, they will be a backbiter, of a restless or fickle intellect, wicked, a performer of sinful deeds, of an unattractive appearance, and will be addicted to the vices of others and inclined toward theft. || 1 ||

A person born in the **second sub-division** will be wealthy and powerful, an enjoyer of life’s pleasures, and will lack any desire for conflict or battle. Such a person is fond of music <note>original: "gandharva," referring to the celestial musicians of the gods</note> and is deeply devoted to women. || 2 ||

One born in the **third sub-division** is very righteous and follows the path of Dharma, yet is constantly afflicted by some form of ailment. They are a knower of the essential truth of all things, possess great wisdom, and are a devoted worshiper of the gods. || 3 ||

If one is born in the **fourth sub-division**, they are noble and "initiated" <note>original: "dikshita," meaning one who has undergone sacred rites or performs Vedic sacrifices</note>, possessing great devotion to their Guru (teacher). Such a person shall obtain whatever wealth or treasures are hidden within the earth. || 4 ||

<summary>This page concludes the traits of the Pisces ascendant and begins detailing the specific personality influences of the first four "Navamshas" (ninth-part sub-divisions) of a birth sign.</summary>
<keywords>Pisces, Meena Lagna, Navamsha, astrological sub-divisions, character traits, hidden treasure, Vedic astrology</keywords>

**One born in the fifth sub-division becomes a famous king, endowed with all auspicious characteristics. He is blessed with a long life and many sons. || 5 ||**

If a person is born in the fifth <term>Navamsha: A 3° 20' sub-division of a zodiac sign</term>, he will have a long lifespan, many sons, and will be a renowned ruler possessing all the marks of greatness. || 5 ||

**One born in the sixth sub-division is conquered by women, lacks auspicious virtues, is overly talkative, and is impotent. He destroys his wealth and is prone to negligence. || 6 ||**

If one is born in the sixth sub-division, he remains under the control of women, is devoid of good deeds, speaks excessively, and is weak or impotent <note>original: "napunsaka"</note>. He wastes his riches and is perpetually careless. || 6 ||

**One born in the seventh sub-division is courageous, wise, and fierce. He remains undefeated in battles, is filled with great enthusiasm, and is content. || 7 ||**

If one takes birth in the seventh sub-division, he will be powerful, intelligent, a brave warrior, victorious in conflicts, highly energetic, and satisfied with what he has. || 7 ||

**One born in the eighth sub-division is ungrateful, envious, and cruel. He suffers much distress, has many offspring, and abandons his work at the most critical moment. || 8 ||**

If one is born in the eighth sub-division, he is ungrateful, jealous, sinful, a sufferer of hardships, and has many children. He is prone to abandoning his efforts just when the results are about to manifest <note>original: "phalakalaparityagi," literally one who discards the time of the fruit/result</note>. || 8 ||

**One born in the ninth sub-division is skilled in his duties, clever, majestic, and has mastered his senses. He is always surrounded by servants. || 9 ||**

He who is born in the ninth sub-division of the birth sign will be proficient in all actions, clever, beautiful, powerful, and a <term>Jitendriya: One who has conquered their physical senses and desires</term>. He is always accompanied by attendants and servants. || 9 ||

### Now, the Results of the Temperament Groups

<note>In Vedic astrology, the "Ganas" categorize human nature into three groups: Deva (Divine), Manushya (Human), and Rakshasa (Demonic), based on the moon's position at birth.</note>

**A person born in the Divine Group is beautiful, charitable, wise, and always strong. He enjoys few worldly pleasures and possesses supreme intelligence. || 1 ||**

<note>original: "Devaganodbhava" — one born under the influence of the Divine temperament.</note>

A man born into the Divine category <note>original: "Deva Gana"</note> is handsome, charitable, intelligent, consistently strong, moderate in his enjoyments, and a great scholar. || 1 ||

> ->A man born of the Human category is self-respecting, wealthy, large-eyed, a skilled archer who hits his mark, fair-complexioned, and a source of joy for his fellow citizens. || 2 ||<-

A man born into the Human category <note>original: "Martya Gana" or "Manushya Gana"</note> is proud, wealthy, has large eyes, is a master of marksmanship and archery, possesses a fair complexion, and brings delight to the people of his town. || 2 ||

> ->A man born in the Demonic category is prone to mania, has a frightening appearance, is always fond of strife, is perpetually miserable, and suffers from urinary disorders. || 3 ||<-

A man born into the Demonic category <note>original: "Rakshasa Gana"</note> is erratic or manic, has a terrifying form, is always fond of quarrels, is constantly unhappy, and suffers from <term>Prameha: A group of urinary ailments in traditional medicine, often associated with diabetes</term>. || 3 ||

### Concerning the Junction Portent (Ganda Yoga) —

> The beginning of Mula, Magha, and Ashvini constellations are three Ganda-nadikas; the ends of Jyeshtha, Ashlesha, and Revati are five Ganda-nadikas. || 1 ||

The first three <term>Ghatis: A traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> of the <term>Nakshatras: Lunar mansions or constellations</term> known as Mula, Magha, and Ashvini are considered the "Gandanta" (the dangerous junction at the beginning). The final five ghatis of the Jyeshtha, Ashlesha, and Revati constellations are considered the "Gandanta" at the end. || 1 ||

### Concerning the Remedy for the Junction Portent —

> A child born in Ganda Yoga during the twilight, night, or day certainly destroys themselves, the mother, or the father, respectively. || 2 ||

> Brihaspati says: there is fear of thieves during travel, and death itself during marriage; such a birth destroys the mother and father. || 3 ||

If a child is born during the Portent Junction (Ganda Yoga) at the time of evening twilight, during the night, or during the day, they will—in that specific order—bring destruction to themselves, their mother, or their father. || 2 ||

Furthermore, Brihaspati <note>The preceptor of the gods and a primary authority in Vedic astrology</note> declares that if this yoga occurs during a journey, there is a danger of thieves; if during a marriage, it leads to death. It is said to be ruinous for both the mother and the father. || 3 ||

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Ganda Yoga" (the junction portent) begun on page 73. It details the specific familial dangers posed by births during these inauspicious times and provides the ritual prescriptions for "Shanti" (pacification) to neutralize these effects.</meta>

<page-num>74</page-num>
# Lagnachandrika

If a child is born during the junction portent <term>Ganda Yoga: A specific inauspicious astrological combination involving the transition between certain lunar mansions</term> in the evening, it brings harm to the child's own body. If born at night, it harms the mother; if born during the day, it harms the father. Undertaking a journey during this junction causes a fear of thieves, and a marriage performed then leads to death. In this same way, the sage Brihaspati <note>The preceptor of the gods and the planet Jupiter in Vedic tradition</note> states that it destroys the parents. || 2-3 ||

> To pacify the Ganda affliction, take neem, sandalwood, Indian costus <note>original: "Kustha"</note>, and bezoar <term>Gorochana: A bright yellow pigment or gallstone found in cattle, highly valued in ritual and medicine</term>. || 4 ||
> These should be mixed with clarified butter <note>original: "ghrita"</note> and placed into four water-pots. Then, a wise person should bathe the child while reciting the "Thousand-Headed Hymn" <note>original: "Sahasra-shirsha mantra," referring to the Purusha Sukta from the Rigveda</note>. || 5 ||
> If born during the day, bathe the child with the father; if at night, with the mother. If born during either of the two twilights (dawn or dusk), bathe the child with both the father and the mother. To pacify the junction, one should donate a bronze vessel filled with clarified butter. || 6 ||
> One should also donate a black cow and gold, and arrange for the chanting of planetary mantras. This same ritual for peace is prescribed for those born in the constellations of Ashlesha and Mula. || 7 ||

If a child is born in the Ganda Yoga, or if one must travel during that time, the remedy <note>original: "shanti"</note> is as follows: mix neem, sandalwood, Indian costus, and bezoar with clarified butter and place them in four water-pots. Then, a learned priest should bathe the child using the Vedic mantras beginning with "The Thousand-Headed Purusha..." <note>original: "Sahasra-shirsha Purushah," the first line of the Purusha Sukta</note>. If the birth occurred during the day, the father should participate in the bath; if at night, the mother should participate. If the child was born during the morning or evening twilight, both parents should participate. To pacify the Ganda, one must donate a bronze vessel filled with clarified butter. One should also donate a black cow and gold, and have the prayers for the planets performed. This ritual for peace is also prescribed for children born under the lunar mansions of <term>Ashlesha: The 9th Nakshatra, associated with serpents</term> and <term>Mula: The 19th Nakshatra, associated with the goddess of destruction</term>. || 4-7 ||

---

1. The specific methods for the peace-ritual for Mula and other constellations can be found in the *Muhurtachintamani* <note>"The Thought-Gem of Auspicious Moments," a famous 16th-century astrological text</note> and its commentary, the *Piyushadhara-tika* <note>"The Stream of Nectar Commentary"</note>.

### Now, the Results of the Sun and Other Planets in their Exaltation Signs

> A person whose Sun <note>original: "Bhaskara"</note> is in exaltation <term>Uccha: The specific zodiac sign where a planet exerts its maximum power</term> becomes very wealthy and possesses a formidable, fierce temperament. A person whose Moon <note>original: "Nishakara"</note> is in exaltation wears beautiful jewelry, experiences great enjoyment, and is wealthy. || 1 ||

A person who has the Sun in its exaltation sign is very wealthy and highly fierce; if the Moon is in its exaltation, they possess beautiful ornaments, are a seeker of pleasures, and are wealthy. || 1 ||

> If Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> is in exaltation, the person will have excellent sons, be radiant, and be proud. If Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> is in exaltation, one becomes intelligent, firm in their speech, and possessed of great strength. || 2 ||

If Mars is in its exaltation sign, one has beautiful sons, is radiant, and is filled with pride. If Mercury is in its exaltation, one is wise, stands by their word, and is endowed with great strength. || 2 ||

> If Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> is exalted, the person is honored by kings, famous, learned, and noble. If Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note> is in its own exaltation, one is fond of luxury, humor, music, and similar pleasures. || 3 ||

If Jupiter is in its exaltation, one is honored by the government, famous, learned, and a noble person. If Venus is in its exaltation, one remains engaged in luxury <gloss>enjoyment</gloss>, humor, and music. || 3 ||

> When the son of the Sun <note>original: "Raviputra," referring to Saturn</note> is in his exaltation sign, one becomes a universal monarch <term>Chakravarti: An emperor whose chariot wheels roll everywhere without obstruction; a world-ruler</term> or is wealthy. One may also receive an official appointment or authority from a king. The results for Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon</note> are considered equal to those of Saturn. || 4 ||

If Saturn is in its exaltation, one becomes a sovereign ruler, a wealthy person, or receives an official position from the king. The fruit of Rahu is considered to be the same as that of Saturn. || 4 ||

---

### Now, the Results of Planets in their Root-Trine Signs

> When the Sun is in its Root-Trine <term>Moolatrikona: A planetary placement second in strength only to exaltation</term>, one is wealthy, happy, and skilled in their work. When the Moon is so placed, one is wealthy and a great enjoyer of life. With Mars, one is a hero, yet merciless and wicked. || 1 ||

# Lagnachandrika
# <note>Moonlight of the Ascendant</note>

If the Sun is in its root-trine <term>Moolatrikona: A specific area of a zodiac sign where a planet is exceptionally strong, though slightly less so than in exaltation</term>, the person will be wealthy, happy, and skilled in their work. If the Moon is in its root-trine, they will be wealthy and fond of life's pleasures. If Mars is there, the person will be a brave warrior, but also merciless and wicked. || 1 ||

> If Mercury is in its trine, the man is wise, humorous, and victorious.
> If Jupiter is there, he becomes the master of villages, cities, and monasteries. || 2 ||

If Mercury is in its root-trine, the person will be a scholar, full of joy, and a victor. If Jupiter is there, they will be the leader or sovereign of a village, a town, or a religious monastery. || 2 ||

> If Venus is in its trine, the person is wise, happy, and superior.
> If Saturn is there, the person is filled with wealth, a great hero, and an upholder of the lineage. || 3 ||

If Venus is in its root-trine, the person is a scholar, endowed with happiness, and is a great and noble individual. If Saturn <note>original: "Manda," meaning the slow-moving one</note> is in its root-trine, they will be overflowing with wealth, a great warrior, and one who brings prosperity and honor to their family. || 3 ||

> Leo, Taurus, Aries, Virgo, Sagittarius, Libra, and Aquarius
> are the root-trines of the Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn respectively. || 4 ||

The signs of Leo, Taurus, Aries, Virgo, Sagittarius, Libra, and Aquarius are called the root-trine <gloss>Moolatrikona</gloss> signs for the Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn, in that specific order. || 4 ||

### Now, the Results of a Planet Situated in its Own House

> When the Sun is in its own house, the person is very fierce and highly industrious in the world.
> When the Moon is there, they are devoted to righteousness, saintly, thoughtful, and handsome. || 1 ||

If the Sun is seated in its own sign <note>Leo</note>, the person will be formidable and extremely hardworking in society. If the Moon is in its own sign <note>Cancer</note>, they will be dedicated to virtue <gloss>Dharma</gloss>, saintly, a deep thinker, and physically beautiful. || 1 ||

> If Mars is in its own house, the person is a wrestler, wealthy, and undefeated.
> If Mercury is there, they are an expert in various arts, a scholar, and a wealthy man. || 2 ||

If Mars is in its own house <term>Swagriha: A zodiac sign ruled by that specific planet, where it feels "at home" and comfortable</term>, the person will be an athlete, wealthy, and undefeated (one who does not lose). ### Now, the results of a planet residing in a friend's house

> When the Sun is in a friend's house, one is famous, a scholar, and a steady friend.
> With the Moon, the man is fortunate, clever, and also wealthy. || 1 ||

If the Sun is in the house of a friend <term>Mitragriha: A zodiac sign ruled by a planet that is naturally or temporarily friendly toward the planet in question</term>, the person will be a famous scholar and a loyal, steady friend. If the Moon is there, they will be fortunate, clever, and wealthy. || 1 ||

> With Mars, one lives by the weapon; with Mercury, one is endowed with beauty and wealth.
> With Jupiter in a friend's house, one is worshipful to the righteous and engaged in good deeds. || 2 ||

If Mars is in a friend's house, the person earns a living through weapons <note>This likely refers to military, police, or security service</note>. If Mercury is there, they are endowed with physical beauty and wealth. If Jupiter is in a friend's house, the person is honored by virtuous people and performs good deeds. || 2 ||

> With Venus in a friend's house, one is wealthy in the world and dear to their kinsmen.
> With Saturn, the man eats the food of others and is intent on bad deeds. || 3 ||

If Venus is in a friend's house, the person is wealthy in society and beloved by their relatives. If Saturn is in a friend's house, the person survives on the food provided by others <note>suggesting dependency or lack of self-sufficiency</note> and is prone to performing bad actions. || 3 ||

# Now, the Results of Planets Residing in an Enemy's House

If the Sun is in an enemy’s house <term>Shatru-griha: A zodiac sign ruled by a planet that is naturally hostile to the planet occupying it</term>, the person is destitute and afflicted by sensual desires. If the Moon is there, they suffer from heart disease, and if Mars is there, they have a foolish wife and are impoverished. || 1 ||

If the Sun is in the house of an enemy, the person will be poor and troubled by worldly subjects/desires. If the Moon is there, they will have disease in the heart, and if Mars is there, they will have a foolish wife and be without wealth. || 1 ||

If Mercury is in an enemy’s house, the person is a fool, wealthy only in words <note>original: "vagdhanī" — meaning one who talks big but has no actual resources</note>, and afflicted by sorrow. If Jupiter is in an enemy’s sign, the man is impotent, his livelihood is destroyed, and he is famished. || 2 ||

If Mercury is in the house of an enemy, the person will be a fool, possessing wealth only in his speech, and will be troubled by grief. If Jupiter is in the house of an enemy, the person will be impotent, devoid of a profession, and hungry. || 2 ||

If Venus is in an enemy’s house, the person is a servant, possesses a wicked intellect, and is miserable. If Saturn is there, he will be distressed by disease and the grief of losing wealth, and he will be unclean. || 3 ||

If Venus is in the house of an enemy, the person will be a servant, have bad intentions, and be unhappy. If Saturn is there, he will be troubled by illness and the sorrow of losing money, and will be physically or morally unclean. || 3 ||

# Now, the Results of Planets Residing in a Sign of Debilitation

If the Sun is in debilitation <term>Neecha: The specific zodiac sign where a planet’s influence is at its weakest and most negative</term>, the man becomes a servant and is abandoned by his relatives. If the Moon is in its sign of debilitation, he is sickly, possesses little merit <note>original: "punya"</note>, and is unfortunate. || 1 ||

If the Sun is debilitated, the person will be one who serves others and will be deserted by his brothers/relatives. If the Moon is in its sign of debilitation, he will be diseased, have very little spiritual merit, and be a beggar. || 1 ||

If Mars is in debilitation, the person will be lowly, contemptible, and overwhelmed by vices. If Mercury is there, he is mean-spirited and an enemy to his own relatives. If Jupiter is there, he is miserable and covered in filth. || 2 ||

# Now, the Second Chapter

->Regarding the results of the Sun being positioned in the twelve houses, starting with the Ascendant.<-

> If the Sun is in the Ascendant, one is extremely fierce, restless in soul, and afflicted by desire.
> One becomes a sufferer of eye diseases and physical pain, and possesses a reddish complexion. || 1 ||

If the Sun is in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth, representing the self and the body</term>, the person possesses an extremely fierce temperament, a restless mind, and is troubled by sensual desires. They suffer from eye ailments and physical afflictions, and have a reddish appearance to their body. || 1 ||

> That man becomes a ruler, compassionate, and of excellent character. || 3 ||

<note>original: "Divanatha" — a poetic title for the Sun, literally meaning "The Lord of the Day"</note> If the Sun is in the third house <note>The house associated with courage, siblings, and prowess</note>, the person becomes famous and enjoys good health. He lives like a king, is full of compassion, and possesses a virtuous character. || 3 ||

If the Sun is in the third house <note>The Third House usually represents siblings, courage, and communication.</note>, the person becomes famous, free from disease, powerful like a king, compassionate, and possesses a beautiful temperament. || 3 ||

> If the Sun is in the fourth house, the person is of evil intellect, emaciated in body, and devoid of happiness. They lack influence, are cruel, and keep the company of wicked people. || 4 ||

If the Sun is in the fourth house <note>The Fourth House represents the home, emotions, and the mother.</note>, the person possesses a wicked mind, a lean or weak physical frame, and lacks happiness. They are without influence, harsh in nature, and live in the company of bad people. || 4 ||

> If the Sun is in the fifth house, one is filled with anger, ill-formed in appearance, and devoid of good character. They earn their living through bad company and become physically wasted. || 5 ||

If the Sun is in the fifth house <note>The Fifth House governs children, intelligence, and past merits.</note>, the person is prone to anger, unattractive, and lacks moral character. They are often thin or emaciated and derive their livelihood from associating with the wrong sort of people. || 5 ||

> If the Sun is in the sixth house, one is free from enemies, famous, honored, happy, and pure. Such a man is brave, affectionate, and respected by the king. || 6 ||

If the Sun is in the sixth house <note>The Sixth House is associated with enemies, debts, and diseases; the Sun here is traditionally seen as "burning away" these obstacles.</note>, the person remains without enemies, is well-known, happy, and pure of heart. They are a brave and loving individual who receives recognition from the government or ruling authorities. || 6 ||

> If the Sun is in the seventh house, one has a wicked spouse, is fond of bad people, and has few sons. The person born under this sign suffers from secret diseases and is prone to sin. || 7 ||

If the Sun is in the seventh house <note>The Seventh House governs marriage and partnerships.</note>, the person has an ill-natured spouse, takes pleasure in the company of the wicked, has few children, suffers from rectal or "secret" ailments <note>original: "guhya-rogi"</note>, and is inclined toward sinful behavior. || 7 ||

> If the Sun—the Lord of the Day—is in the eighth house, one is ungrateful, of a lowly mind, scorched by enemies, a wanderer without purpose, and devoid of kinsmen. || 8 ||

If the Sun is in the eighth house <note>The Eighth House relates to longevity, secrets, and obstacles.</note>, the person is ungrateful, harbors base thoughts, is troubled or "burned" by enemies, travels aimlessly, and is abandoned by their relatives. || 8 ||

> If the Sun is positioned in the ninth house, the one born is a doer of bad deeds and is devoid of good fortune. They lack both knowledge and discernment and are of bad character. || 9 ||

<summary>This page describes the astrological effects of the Sun when placed in the third through ninth houses, ranging from the auspicious "destroyer of enemies" in the sixth house to the more difficult placements in the fourth, seventh, and eighth houses.</summary>
<keywords>Sun, Surya, Astrological Houses, Lagnachandrika, Jyotish, Third House, Sixth House, Ninth House, planetary effects</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the astrological analysis of the Sun's placement in the twelve houses, specifically covering the ninth through twelfth houses, and transitions into the effects of the Moon's placement starting from the first house (the Ascendant).</meta>

<page-num>6, 81</page-num>
<header>Including Commentary in the Common Language</header>

If the Sun is in the ninth house <note>The Ninth House, or *Dharma Bhava*, governs fortune, spirituality, and fatherhood.</note>, the person becomes a wrongdoer, luckless, devoid of wisdom and discernment, and possesses a wicked nature. || 9 ||

**If the Sun is in the tenth house, the person is born devoid of relatives, a wrongdoer, and lacking in moral character. They are fickle toward women, lacking in luster, and devoid of financial reserves.** || 10 ||
If the Sun is in the tenth house <note>The Tenth House represents career, social status, and public life.</note>, the individual is separated from their kin, performs bad deeds, lacks character, is restless in the company of women, lacks vitality <note>original Sanskrit: *hīnatejā*</note>, and is without wealth. || 10 ||

**If the Sun is in the house of gains, the one born is endowed with various profits. They are virtuous, religious, honorable, and also handsome.** || 11 ||
If the Sun is in the eleventh house <note>The Eleventh House, or *Labha Bhava*, governs income, gains, and friendships.</note>, the child born is blessed with many kinds of profit, possesses a <term>Sattvic|A quality of purity, goodness, and harmony</term> nature, is religious, respected, and physically attractive. || 11 ||

**If the Sun is in the house of expenditure, the man is diseased, devoid of strength, and an aimless wanderer. He spends on unworthy things and is devoid of devotion to his son and wife.** || 12 ||
If the Sun is in the twelfth house <note>The Twelfth House, or *Vyaya Bhava*, represents loss, expenses, and isolation.</note>, the person is sickly, lacks strength, wanders fruitlessly, <unclear>wastefully</unclear> spends his resources, and lacks devotion. || 12 ||

### Now, the effects of the Moon in the twelve houses starting from the Ascendant

**If the Moon is in the Ascendant, the man is dull, pure, cheerful, and filled with wealth. He is beloved by women, religious, and yet ungrateful.** || 1 ||
If the Moon is in the first house <note>The Ascendant or *Lagna* represents the self, physical body, and personality.</note>, the person may be slow-witted <note>original: *jada* — can mean dull or heavy-minded</note>, but is pure-hearted, happy, wealthy, a favorite of women, religious, and ungrateful. || 1 ||

**If the Moon is in the house of wealth, he is full of riches, honored by the king, and endowed with virtues. He is a lover of scriptures, beautiful, and gains the affection of the people.** || 2 ||
If the Moon is in the second house <note>The Second House, or *Dhana Bhava*, governs wealth, speech, and family.</note>, the person is filled with wealth, respected by the sovereign, virtuous, a lover of the sacred sciences <note>original: *shastranuragi*</note>, and is loved by beautiful people. || 2 ||

<summary>This page concludes the descriptions of the Sun's influence in the final astrological houses (9-12) and introduces the effects of the Moon's placement in the first and second houses.</summary>

> **If the Lord of the Night <note>original Sanskrit: *Nishānātha*, a poetic name for the Moon</note> is in the third house, the person is endowed with wealth, knowledge, and other virtues. They have an excess of phlegm <note>In Ayurvedic medicine, an excess of *Kapha* (phlegm) relates to a specific bodily constitution</note>, are lustful, and become the leader of their lineage.** || 3 ||

If the Moon is in the third house <note>The Third House governs siblings, courage, and communication</note>, then the person is joined with wealth and education, possesses much phlegm, is passionate, and is a chief member of their family. || 3 ||

> **If the Lord of the Night is in the fourth house, the person is accompanied by sons and a spouse. They are remembered as being wealthy, happy, famous, and also learned.** || 4 ||

If the Moon is in the fourth house <note>The Fourth House, or *Matru Bhava*, governs the mother, home, and happiness</note>, the living being is joined by sons and a wife, and becomes wealthy, happy, renowned, and knowledgeable. || 4 ||

> **If the Moon is in the house of the son <note>The Fifth House, or *Putra Bhava*, which governs children and intelligence</note>, the man has many sons, is sickly, lustful, and formidable. He is endowed with artificial prowess <note>original Sanskrit: *kṛtrimaiḥ pauruṣair*, suggesting an outward show of strength or efforts that are not entirely natural</note> and is humble.** || 5 ||

If the Moon is in the fifth house, the person is endowed with sons, but is sickly, passionate, intimidating, possesses artificial manliness, and is well-behaved. || 5 ||

> **If the Moon is in the sixth house, the person is devoid of wealth, has a delicate body, and is extremely greedy. They have <term>weak digestion|original: mandāgni, literally 'slow fire'</term>, a piercing gaze, and a sinful mind.** || 6 ||

If the Moon is in the sixth house <note>The Sixth House, or *Shatru Bhava*, governs enemies, debts, and diseases</note>, one becomes poor, has a soft body, is very covetous, has poor digestion, a sharp look, and a wicked intellect. || 6 ||

> **If a person is born with the Moon in the seventh house, they are sorrowful, <term>leprous|In ancient texts, 'kushthi' often refers to a variety of chronic skin conditions</term>, and a deceiver. They are a miser with many enemies and enjoy the wives of others.** || 7 ||

If the Moon is in the seventh house <note>The Seventh House, or *Kalatra Bhava*, governs marriage and partnerships</note>, one is miserable, a leper, a cheat, stingy, has many foes, and is a philanderer <note>original Hindi: *paradarika*, one who pursues others' wives</note>. || 7 ||

> **If the Lord of the Stars <note>original Sanskrit: *Tārakānātha*, another epithet for the Moon</note> is in the eighth house, the person is wretched, short-lived, and full of suffering. They are impudent, lean-bodied, and have a sinful mind.** || 8 ||

If the Moon is in the eighth house <note>The Eighth House, or *Ayur Bhava*, governs longevity and mysteries</note>, one is destitute (poor), has a short life, is miserable, <term>bold|original: pragalbha, which can mean daring but often implies impudence or arrogance in this context</term>, thin, and possesses a sinful mind. || 8 ||

> **If the Moon is in the ninth house, the person possesses a beautiful radiance, remains always devoted to their own faith, is free from disease, is praised by the virtuous, and is born without sin.** || 9 ||

> **When the Nectar-Rayed One <note>original Sanskrit: *Sudhārashmi*, a poetic name for the Moon referring to its cool, life-giving light</note> is in the tenth house, the person is very fortunate, immensely wealthy, wise, charming, and honored by royalty.** || 10 ||

If the Moon is in the tenth house <note>The Tenth House, or *Karma Bhava*, governs profession and public status</note>, the person will be very lucky, extremely rich, intellectual, attractive, and respected by the government or kings. || 10 ||

If the Moon is in the eleventh house <note>The Eleventh House, or *Labha Bhava*, governs income, gains, and elder siblings</note>, the person is possessed of gains, is bold (or daring), prosperous, walks the path of virtue, is shy or modest, powerful, and lucky. || 11 ||

> **If the Moon is in the house of loss, the person is of sinful intellect, a glutton, frequently defeated, the lowest of their lineage, a drinker of intoxicants, and subject to physical or mental disorders.** || 12 ||

If the Moon is in the twelfth house <note>The Twelfth House, or *Vyaya Bhava*, governs expenses, isolation, and the subconscious</note>, the person will have a wicked mind, eat excessively, be prone to losing, be the most degraded in their family, be a consumer of wine, and be afflicted by various ailments. || 12 ||

### Now, the results of Mars in the twelve houses starting from the Ascendant

> **If Mars is in the Ascendant, the person is unattractive, sickly, abandoned by relatives, a liar, devoid of wealth, and pursues the spouses of others.** || 13 ||

If Mars <note>original Sanskrit: *Mangala* or *Bhauma*</note> is in the first house <note>The Ascendant or *Lagna* represents the physical body and personality</note>, the individual is ill-formed, prone to illness, lacks the support of kin, speaks untruths, is poor, and is prone to adultery. || 13 ||

> **If Mars is in the house of wealth, the man is devoid of riches and lacks the drive for action. He is a procrastinator, yet remains truthful and possesses sons.** || 14 ||

<note>The second house governs wealth, speech, and family</note>
<term>Procrastinator</term>: The original Sanskrit term is *dirghasutri*, which literally means "one who spins a long thread," describing someone who takes a long time to complete simple tasks or habitually delays.

->**With the Son of the Earth in the fourth, one is dark-complexioned,**<-

->**lacks children, and is intensely lustful. || 16 ||**<-

->**is always sickly, detached from their circle of kin,**<-
->**and is a man devoid of intellect. || 17 ||**<-

->**With the Son of the Earth in the seventh, one is blood-stained,**<-
->**prone to anger, a servant of the base, a deceiver,**<-
->**and may even be a man devoid of merit. || 19 ||**<-

->**With Mars in the eighth, one is afflicted by skin disease,**<-
->**short-lived, and tormented by enemies; possessing little wealth,**<-
->**afflicted by illness, and born without virtue. || 20 ||**<-

If Mars is in the eighth house <note>The Eighth House, or *Ashtama Bhava*, is traditionally associated with longevity, secrets, and chronic illness</note>, the person suffers from skin diseases <note>original: *kusthi*, which can refer to leprosy or general skin ailments</note>, has a short lifespan, is harassed by enemies, possesses little wealth, is prone to illness, and lacks virtuous qualities. || 20 ||

->**When the Son of the Earth is in the house of action, one performs auspicious deeds and is filled with grace;**<-

If Mars is in the twelfth house <note>The Twelfth House, or *Vyaya Bhava*, relates to expenditure, loss, and foreign travel</note>, the person spends wealth on bad causes, is an unbeliever, harsh, foolish, has many enemies, and always travels abroad. || 24 ||

### Now, the results of Mercury in the twelve houses, beginning with the Ascendant

->**With Mercury in the Ascendant, one is knowledgeable in song, sinless, and honored by the king;**<-
->**endowed with beauty, wisdom, and fame, the person becomes quite bold. || 1 ||**<-

If Mercury <note>original: *Budha*</note> is in the first house <note>The First House, or *Lagna*, represents the physical body, temperament, and self</note>, the person is a singer or musician, free from sin, honored by the ruler, handsome, possessed of wisdom and fame, and is very eloquent. || 1 ||

<meta>This page continues the sequence of planetary placements throughout the houses of the zodiac. This section transitions from the second house through to the seventh house.</meta>

<page-num>86</page-num>
<header>Moonlight of the Ascendant</header>

> **When the Son of the Moon is in the house of wealth, one is filled with riches and grain;**
> **Performing auspicious deeds, they are eternally happy and honored by kings. || 2 ||**
<note>original Sanskrit: *Dhanabhāve candraputre...*</note>

If <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: *Budha*. In Vedic astrology, Mercury represents the intellect, speech, and commerce.</note> is in the second house <note>The *Dhana Bhava* or house of wealth and resources.</note>, the person will be abundant in wealth and food-grains, a doer of good deeds, constantly happy, and respected by royalty. || 2 ||

> **Born with Mercury in the third house, one is excellent and honored by kin;**
> **A standard-bearer of righteousness, famous, and a worshipper of gurus and gods. || 3 ||**
<note>original Sanskrit: *Tṛtīyasthe budhe jātaḥ...*</note>

If Mercury is in the third house <note>The *Sahaja Bhava*, associated with siblings, courage, and communication.</note>, the person becomes an excellent individual, respected by their relatives, a champion of religious principles <note>literally "one who holds the flag of Dharma."</note>, renowned, and a devotee of teachers and the divine. || 3 ||

> **With the Son of the Moon in the fourth house, one has many servants and fame;**
> **Eloquent in speech, possessed of good fortune, and a speaker of truth. || 4 ||**
<note>original Sanskrit: *Caturthe candraputre ca...*</note>

If Mercury is in the fourth house <note>The *Sukha Bhava*, representing the home, emotions, and comforts.</note>, the person will have many attendants, possess great fame, be a clever speaker, be endowed with luck, and always speak the truth. || 4 ||

> **With the Son of Rohini in the fifth house, one is blessed with sons and grandsons;**
> **Of fine intellect and virtuous strength, that person becomes happy. || 5 ||**
<note>original Sanskrit: *Pañcame rohiṇīputre...* Rohini-putra is a poetic name for Mercury, referring to the moon's favorite constellation, Rohini.</note>

If Mercury is in the fifth house <note>The *Putra Bhava*, the house of children, intelligence, and creativity.</note>, the person is blessed with children and grandchildren, possesses a beautiful intellect, is full of vital strength <note>original: *sattva*, meaning purity or inner strength.</note>, and lives a happy life. || 5 ||

> **With Mercury in the sixth house, one is cruel and an opponent to all kin;**
> **Highly envious and devoted to lust, even if they are a learned man. || 6 ||**
<note>original Sanskrit: *Ṣaṣṭhe budhe nṛśaṃsaśca...*</note>

If Mercury is in the sixth house <note>The *Shatru Bhava*, the house of enemies, debts, and illness. Mercury’s placement here is often considered difficult for social relations.</note>, the person born will be cruel, a hater of all their relatives, excessively envious, preoccupied with carnal desires, and yet may still be a scholar. || 6 ||

> **With the Son of Soma in the seventh house, a man has great beauty and learning;**
> **Of good character, an expert in the scriptures of love, and honored by women. || 7 ||**
<note>original Sanskrit: *Saptamasthe somaputre...* Soma-putra is another name for Mercury, "Son of the Moon."</note>

If Mercury is in the seventh house <note>The *Yuvati Bhava*, the house of marriage and partnerships.</note>, the person will possess exceptional beauty and high education, have a virtuous character, be well-versed in the <term>Kama Shastra</term> <note>Ancient Indian texts on the art of love and desire.</note>, and be highly regarded by women. || 7 ||

If Mercury is in the eleventh house <note>The *Labha Bhava*, the house of gains and social circles.</note>, one enjoys constant profit, remains free from disease, stays perpetually happy, maintains a loving disposition toward others, and becomes renowned. || 11 ||

<note>original Sanskrit: *Budhe vyaye vyayī loke...*</note>

If Mercury is in the twelfth house <note>The *Vyaya Bhava*, the house of expenditure and loss.</note>, the person spends their wealth freely in the world, is prone to illness, remains among relatives, is attracted to sinful deeds, lives in dependence on others, and favors the side of their opponents. || 12 ||

### Now, the results of Jupiter in the twelve houses starting from the Ascendant

->**With Jupiter in the Ascendant, one is well-behaved, bold, and handsome;**<-
->**A favorite of kings, free from disease, wise, and gentle. || 1 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Lagne gurau suśīlaśca...*</note>

If <term>Jupiter</term> <note>Guru or Brihaspati, the planet of wisdom, expansion, and spirituality.</note> is in the first house <note>The *Lagna* or Ascendant, representing the self and physical body.</note>, the person is of excellent character, mature and confident, physically attractive, favored by the government or royalty, healthy, knowledgeable, and of a mild, pleasing temperament. || 1 ||

<page-num>15</page-num>
<header>Moonlight of the Ascendant</header>

If <term>Jupiter</term> <note>Bṛhaspati</note> is in the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>Lagna, the first house representing the self and physical body</note>, the person possesses a beautiful character, is bold, handsome, respected by the king, free of disease, wise, and gentle. || 1 ||

->**When Jupiter is in the house of wealth, the person is rich, grateful, and joined by kin;**<-
->**Possessing elephants, horses, and buffaloes, they are also born with a radiant glow. || 2 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Dhane jīve dhanī lokaḥ...*</note>

If Jupiter is in the second house <note>The *Dhana Bhava*, associated with wealth and speech.</note>, the person is wealthy, grateful, surrounded by family members, and owns livestock such as elephants, horses, and buffaloes <note>In historical contexts, these were primary indicators of high status and agricultural wealth.</note>, and possesses a radiant appearance. || 2 ||

->**With Jupiter in the third, one is energetic, clever in work, and self-controlled;**<-
->**Endowed with happiness from friends and relatives, they are fond of visiting holy sites. || 3 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Jīve tṛtīye tejasvī...*</note>

If Jupiter is in the third house <note>The *Sahaja Bhava*, relating to siblings, courage, and short journeys.</note>, the person is energetic, skilled in their duties, <gloss>one who has mastered their senses</gloss>, enjoys happiness derived from friends, and finds great joy in going on <term>pilgrimages</term> <note>Tīrthayātrā</note>. || 3 ||

->**With Jupiter in the house of happiness, one is happy in the world, fortunate, and honored by the king;**<-
->**A conqueror of enemies and head of their clan, they are devoted to their teacher. || 4 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Sukhe jīve sukhī loke...*</note>

If Jupiter is in the fourth house <note>The *Sukha Bhava*, representing the home, mother, and general contentment.</note>, the person is happy in this world, prosperous, respected by the sovereign, victorious over their foes, the leader of their family, and remains devoted to their <term>Guru</term> <note>Teacher or spiritual preceptor</note>. || 4 ||

->**With Jupiter in the house of progeny, one has children, is religious, scholarly, and happy;**<-
->**Possessing a pure heart and compassion, the man is humble. || 5 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Sute jīve sutairyukto...*</note>

If Jupiter is in the fifth house <note>The *Suta Bhava*, the house of children, creativity, and intelligence.</note>, the person is blessed with children, is pious, a learned scholar, happy, pure-minded, full of compassion, and modest in nature. || 5 ||

->**With Jupiter in the sixth, one is beset by obstacles, many enemies, and is harsh;**<-
->**Anxious, lacking in wisdom, and overcome by lust. || 6 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Ṣaṣṭhe gurau vighnayukto...*</note>

If Jupiter is in the sixth house <note>The *Shatru Bhava*, dealing with enemies, debts, and illness.</note>, the person faces many obstacles, has numerous enemies, is cruel, constantly anxious, lacks intelligence, and is driven by carnal desires. || 6 ||

->**When the Teacher of the Gods is in the seventh, the mind is filled with desire, yet they are very strong;**<-
->**Wealthy, charitable, eloquent, and skilled in various arts. || 7**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Saptamasthe surācārye...* "Surācārye" is a title for Jupiter meaning 'Teacher of the Deities.'</note>

<note>The Hindi commentary for verse 7 is partially missing or truncated in the original manuscript but follows the Sanskrit verse above: If Jupiter is in the seventh house (the house of marriage and partnerships), the person is preoccupied with desire, possesses great strength, is wealthy, generous, confident/eloquent, and skilled in <term>varied works</term> <note>Citrakarmā, which can refer to painting or multifaceted talents.</note>.</note>

<summary>This page details the astrological effects of Jupiter (Bṛhaspati) as it resides in the first seven houses of a birth chart. While generally considered a benevolent planet bringing wealth and wisdom, its placement in the sixth house is noted as a source of obstacles and anxiety.</summary>
<keywords>Jupiter, Bṛhaspati, Guru, Astrology, Lagnachandrika, Vedic Houses, Ascendant, Wealth, Pilgrimage, Progeny</keywords>

<header>With Commentary in the Common Language</header>

If <term>Jupiter</term> <note>Bṛhaspati</note> is in the seventh house, the person is passionate, exceptionally strong, wealthy, charitable, bold <note>The Hindi text uses "dhīṭh," implying a certain daring or stubbornness</note>, and a performer of extraordinary or unique deeds. || 7 ||

->**When Jupiter is in the eighth, one is always sickly, miserly, and filled with grief;**<-
->**Having many enemies, performing bad deeds, and being unattractive in form. || 8 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Jīve’ṣṭame sadā rogī kṛpaṇaḥ śokasaṃyutaḥ...*</note>

If Jupiter is in the eighth house, the person is constantly plagued by illness, is a miser, is burdened by sorrow, has many enemies, and possesses an unattractive appearance. || 8 ||

->**With Jupiter in the ninth, one performs righteous deeds, keeps holy company, and knows the scriptures;**<-
->**Humble, a visitor of shrines, and a knower of the Absolute Truth. || 9 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Dharme jīve dharmakartā sādhusaṅgī ca śāstravit...*</note>

If Jupiter is in the ninth house <note>The *Dharma Bhava*, governing religion and fortune</note>, the person associates with saints, understands the sacred <term>shastras</term>, is humble, frequents holy pilgrimage sites, and attains knowledge of <term>Brahman</term> <note>The ultimate, universal reality in Hindu philosophy</note>. || 9 ||

->**When Jupiter enters the house of action, one is endowed with merit, fame, and happiness;**<-
->**Like a king, beautiful in form, and compassionate is that man. || 10 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Karmabhāvagate jīve puṇyakīrtisukhānvitaḥ...*</note>

If Jupiter is in the tenth house <note>The *Karma Bhava*, relating to career and public life</note>, the person is filled with religious merit, fame, and joy. They possess a regal bearing and beauty, and are naturally compassionate. || 10 ||

->**In the house of gain, Jupiter makes one wise, possessing wealth of elephants and horses;**<-
->**Free from greed, beautiful in form, and also virtuous. || 11 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Lābhe gurau vivekī syāddhastyaśvādighanairyutaḥ...*</note>

If Jupiter is in the eleventh house <note>The *Labha Bhava*, governing income and gains</note>, the person is a person of discernment, possesses great wealth such as elephants and horses <note>Traditional symbols of high status and prosperity</note>, is free from excessive desire, is handsome, and full of good qualities. || 11 ||

->**With Jupiter in the house of loss, one is sickly, prone to vice, and follows the faiths of others;**<-
->**Hostile to kin, serving the lowly, and a hater of the spiritual teacher. || 12 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Vyaye bṛhaspatau rogī vyasanī paradharmakṛt...*</note>

If Jupiter is in the twelfth house <note>The *Vyaya Bhava*, relating to expenditures and endings</note>, the person is sickly and prone to addictions. They may follow foreign religions, quarrel with their relatives, serve those of low character, and harbor ill-will toward their <term>Guru</term>. || 12 ||

### Now, the results of Venus in the twelve houses starting from the Ascendant

->**With Venus in the Ascendant, one is of good character, has a livelihood, and is beautiful;**<-
->**Pure, learned, charming, and righteous is that man. || 1 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Lagne śukre suśīlaśca vṛttimānapi sundaraḥ...*</note>

<note>The list of technical terms includes: Bṛhaspati (Jupiter), Śukra (Venus), Bhāva (House/State), Phala (Fruit/Result), Jīve/Gurau (Terms for Jupiter), Vyaye (In the 12th house), Dharme (In the 9th house), Karmabhāva (10th house), Lābhe (11th house), Lagne (Ascendant), and Ṭīkā (Commentary).</note>

<summary>This page describes the astrological effects of Jupiter in the 7th through 12th houses and introduces the effects of Venus in the 1st house. It contrasts the auspicious results of Jupiter in the 9th, 10th, and 11th houses with the negative influences found in the 8th and 12th houses.</summary>

<header># The Moonlight of the Ascendant</header>

If <term>Venus</term> <note>Shukra</note> is in the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>the first house or *Lagna*, representing the self and physical body</note>, the person possesses a beautiful and virtuous character, a good livelihood, is handsome, pure, learned, charming, and religious. || 1 ||

->**When Venus is in the house of wealth, one is wealthy, learned, respected by kin, and honored by kings;**<-
->**A famous man, a devotee of his teachers, and deeply grateful. || 2 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Dhane śukre dhanī vidvānbandhumānyo nṛpārcitaḥ...*</note>

If Venus is in the second house <note>the *Dhana Bhava*, governing wealth and speech</note>, the person will be wealthy, learned, respected by their relatives, honored by the ruler, famous, a devotee of their <term>guru</term>, and full of gratitude. || 2 ||

->**One born with Venus in the third house is endowed with wealth, grain, and children;**<-
->**Free from disease, honored by the king, and possessing great majesty. || 3 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Bhārgave sahaje jāto dhanadhānyasutānvitaḥ...*</note>

If Venus is in the third house <note>the *Sahaja Bhava*, governing siblings and courage</note>, the person is endowed with wealth, food-grains, and children; they are healthy, respected by the state, and influential. || 3 ||

->**With Venus in the house of happiness, one is happy, wise, has many wives, and is filled with wealth;**<-
->**A leader of the village, famous, and a man of great discernment. || 4 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Sukhe śukre sukhī vijño bahubhāryo dhanānvitaḥ...*</note>

If Venus is in the fourth house <note>the *Sukha Bhava*, governing the home, mother, and happiness</note>, the person will be happy, a scholar, possess many wives <note>In historical contexts, this often indicated high social status or virility</note>, be wealthy, a headman of their village, famous, and possess a discerning mind. || 4 ||

->**With Venus in the house of children, a man is always prosperous and beautiful;**<-
->**Endowed with sons, daughters, and grandsons, he is a man of great fortune. || 5 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Sute śukre samṛddhaśca surūpo’pi sadā naraḥ...*</note>

If Venus is in the fifth house <note>the *Suta Bhava*, governing children and intelligence</note>, the person is prosperous, handsome, well-formed, and blessed with children and grandchildren, possessing great opulence. || 5 ||

->**In the sixth house, Venus makes one a hypocrite, prone to dullness and fearlessness;**<-
->**Always a companion of the wicked, a seeker of quarrels, and a hater of his own father. || 6 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Ṣaṣṭhe śukro bhaveddambhī jāḍyahānibhayānvitaḥ...*</note>

If Venus is in the sixth house <note>traditionally a "dusthana" or difficult house associated with enemies and illness</note>, the person is a hypocrite, foolish, lacks a healthy sense of fear <note>implying recklessness</note>, keeps bad company, is argumentative, and always bears ill-will toward their father. || 6 ||

->**In the seventh, the Son of Bhrigu makes one wealthy and joined with a divine wife;**<-
->**Healthy, endowed with happiness, and born with great good fortune. || 7 ||**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: *Saptame bhṛguputre syāddhanī divyāṅganāyutaḥ...*</note>

<summary>This page details the astrological predictions for Venus (Shukra) as it resides in the first seven houses of a birth chart, generally describing positive outcomes for wealth and family, except in the sixth house.</summary>

If Venus is in the seventh house <note>the *Yuvati Bhava*, governing marriage and partnerships</note>, the person will be wealthy, joined with an excellent wife, free from disease, endowed with happiness, and very fortunate. || 7 ||

> If the <term>Worshipped of the Daityas</term> <note>Sanskrit: *daityapūjye*, an epithet for Venus as the preceptor of the titans</note> is located in the eighth house, the person is sickly, fond of quarreling, wanders aimlessly, is idle in their work, yet is considered dear to the people. || 8 ||

If Venus is in the eleventh house, there is always gain; the person is possessed of fame, jewels, and good qualities; they are wealthy, enjoy life’s comforts, act with purity, and are the best among men. || 11 ||

> If Venus is in the <term>house of loss</term> <note>the twelfth house</note>, the person is extravagant, at odds with their teachers and friends, a speaker of lies, and even among their kinsmen, they are born without virtue. || 12 ||

If Venus is in the twelfth house, the person is very spendthrift, creates enmity with their <term>guru</term> <note>teacher or spiritual guide</note> and friends, is a liar, and is considered worthless among their relatives. || 12 ||

<header>Moonlight of the Ascendant</header>

### Now, the Results of Saturn Situated in the Twelve Houses from the Ascendant

->If Saturn is in the Ascendant, a person is always sickly, misshapen, and miserly.<-
->He is ill-mannered, possesses a sinful mind, and certainly becomes dull-witted. || 1 ||<-

If Saturn is in the first house <note>the *Lagna*, representing the self and physical body</note>, the person will always be diseased, ugly, a miser, of bad character, have evil thoughts, and be a fool. || 1 ||

->If the Slow-Moving One is in the house of wealth, one is devoid of riches and afflicted by the wind, bile, and phlegm.<-
->He suffers from diseases of the bones and bile, and possesses very few virtues. || 2 ||<-

If Saturn <note>referred to here as *Manda*, "the Slow-Moving One"</note> is in the second house, the person is poor, suffering from an imbalance of the three humors <term>Vata, Pitta, and Kapha</term> <note>the biological energies in Ayurvedic medicine</note>, suffers from bone ailments and gall-bladder issues, and has little merit. || 2 ||

->If the Son of Chhaya is in the third house, the person is cheerful and a lover of virtue.<-
->He is a crusher of enemies, respected by men, wealthy, and brave. || 3 ||<-

If Saturn <note>referred to by the epithet *Chhayatmaja*, the "Son of Shadow"</note> is in the third house, the person will be happy, a destroyer of enemies, honored by others, wealthy, and a warrior. || 3 ||

->If the Slow-Moving One is in the house of happiness, the person is devoid of joy and has his wealth stolen by relatives.<-
->He is of a virtuous nature but undoubtedly keeps company with wicked people. || 4 ||<-

If Saturn is in the fourth house <note>the *Sukha Bhava*, governing home and happiness</note>, the person is unhappy and has his wealth taken away by his kinsmen—meaning his brothers may seize his property. He may have a virtuous temperament, but he associates with bad company. || 4 ||

->If the Slow-Moving One is in the house of children, he is childless and devoid of noble deeds or fame.<-
->He is without treasure, misshapen, and certainly full of pride. || 5 ||<-

If Saturn is in the fifth house <note>the *Putra Bhava*, governing intelligence and progeny</note>, the person will have no children, will lack a reputation for good works, will be without money, and will be physically unattractive. || 5 ||

->If the Slow-Moving One is situated in the house of enemies, he is free of foes and very wealthy.<-
->The man is endowed with livestock, children, and fame, and remains free of disease. || 6 ||<-

<note>original vocabulary: *Lagne Shanau* (Saturn in the first house), *Dhane Mande* (Saturn in the wealth house), *Tritiyasthe* (situated in the third), *Sukhe Mande* (Saturn in the fourth), *Putre Mande* (Saturn in the fifth), *Shatrubhavasthite* (situated in the sixth house), *Lagnachandrika* (title of the work), *Shaniphalam* (the results of Saturn).</note>

<summary>This page details the astrological implications of Saturn (Shani) being placed in the first through sixth houses of a birth chart. While generally considered a "malefic" planet bringing disease or poverty in the early houses, its placement in the third and sixth houses is described as highly auspicious for wealth and victory.</summary>
<keywords>Saturn, Shani, houses of the horoscope, Vedic astrology, Lagna Chandrika, health and wealth, Ayurvedic humors</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the list of effects for Saturn (Shani) as it moves through the twelve astrological houses, picking up from the sixth house. The previous page covered the first five houses.</meta>

<header>Moonlight of the Ascendant: Including the Hindi Commentary</header>

If Saturn is in the sixth house, the person will be without enemies, very wealthy, and possessed of livestock, sons, and fame, while remaining free from disease. || 6 ||

> ->If the Son of the Sun is in the house of the spouse, one has a wife but is afflicted by illness.<-
> ->He has many enemies, is pale in complexion, thin, and of a dirty appearance. || 7 ||<-

If Saturn is in the seventh house <note>the *Kalatra-bhava*, governing marriage and partnerships</note>, the person remains sickly despite having a wife; he will have many enemies, a poor complexion, and be weak and unkempt. || 7 ||

> ->If the Slow-Moving One is in the eighth, one is prone to anger, poor, and suffers many diseases.<-
> ->He is a creator of false disputes and becomes a person afflicted by wind-based ailments. || 8 ||<-

If Saturn is in the eighth house, the person is easily angered, impoverished, suffers from many illnesses, engages in dishonest arguments, and suffers from <term>Vata</term> <note>original: "Vata-rogi"; an imbalance of the "wind" or "air" humor in Ayurvedic medicine, often relating to joint pain or nervous disorders</note>. || 8 ||

> ->If the Slow-Moving One is in the house of fortune, one is devoid of righteousness, yet discerning, though under the control of enemies.<-
> ->He becomes a cruel person in the world, always devoted to the wives of others. || 9 ||<-

If Saturn is in the ninth house <note>the *Dharma-sthana*, governing religion, luck, and ethics</note>, the person is devoid of religious merit, yet knowledgeable <note>original: "Gyani" or "Viveki", implying a cold or worldly intellect</note>, falls under the control of enemies, is cruel, and is always pursuing other men's wives. || 9 ||

> ->If the Son of the Sun is in the house of action, he performs wicked deeds and is devoid of wealth.<-
> ->He is devoid of the virtues of compassion and truth, and remains always restless. || 10 ||<-

If Saturn is in the tenth house <note>the *Karma-bhava*, governing career and public life</note>, the person is a doer of evil, lacks wealth, is devoid of compassion, truth, and good qualities, and remains constantly fickle. || 10 ||

> ->If the Son of Shadow is in the house of gains, he is an expert in all branches of learning.<-
> ->He is filled with donkeys, camels, and buffaloes, is honored by the king, but remains impure. || 11 ||<-

If Saturn is in the eleventh house <note>the *Labha-sthana*, governing profit and income</note>, the person is proficient in all sciences, possesses many donkeys, camels, and buffaloes <note>representing wealth in terms of transport and draft animals</note>, is respected by the government or king, but remains personally unhygienic. || 11 ||

> ->If the Slow-Moving One is in the house of loss, he spends on ignoble things, is ungrateful, and devoid of wealth.<-
> ->An enemy to his kin, he is poorly dressed and remains always a restless man. || 12 ||<-

If Saturn is in the twelfth house, the person spends money fruitlessly, is ungrateful, lacks wealth, is an enemy to his relatives, wears bad clothes, and is always restless. The results for Rahu and Ketu <note>the North and South Nodes of the Moon</note> are considered the same as those of Saturn. || 12 ||

--- Page 98 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the specific planetary effects of Saturn in the houses (discussed on page 93) to a new section in the treatise. Chapter Three begins here, introducing the "Nara Chakra" or Human Chart, a method of predictive astrology that maps the 27 lunar mansions (Nakshatras) onto the physical form of a human figure relative to the Sun's position.</meta>

<page-num>94</page-num>
<header>Moonlight of the Ascendant</header>

## Now, the Third Chapter [ 3 ]

->First, the Human Chart<-

One should draw the figure of a man <note>original: "Nara-chakra"; a diagram used to determine the influence of the stars based on where they "land" on the body</note> and determine where the Sun is situated. Starting with that constellation, place three stars on the head. || 1 ||

Place three on the face, one on each of the two shoulders, one on each of the two upper arms, and one on each of the two hands. || 2 ||

Place five stars on the heart, one on the navel, and one on the private parts. One should be placed on each of the two knees. || 3 ||

The wise person should place the remaining six constellations on the feet. One counts from the constellation where the Sun is located to the constellation of the person's birth. || 4 ||

After drawing the chart in the shape of a man, take the constellation (Nakshatra) where the Sun is currently placed as the starting point and place three constellations on the head. Place three on the mouth, one on each shoulder, one on each arm, and one on each hand. Place five constellations on the heart, just one on the navel, and one on the anus. Place one on each of the two knees. Then, place the remaining six constellations on the feet. Finally, count from the Sun's constellation to the individual's birth constellation <note>the *Janma-Nakshatra*, or the moon's position at the time of birth</note> to see where it falls on the body. || 1-4 ||

->Now, the Sun Chart<-

If the birth constellation is situated on the head, the man will be an official of the state. If it is in the mouth, he will be a consumer of sweet foods. If it falls on the shoulders, he will be a rider of elephants. || 1 ||

If the constellation is on the head, the person becomes a <term>Pattabandhi</term> <note>original: "Pattabandhi"; literally "one who ties the silk band," meaning a high-ranking official, a crown-wearer, or someone in direct royal service</note>. If it falls in the mouth, he will enjoy delicious sweets. If it falls on the two shoulders, he will be a <term>Gajavahana</term> <note>original: "Gaja-vahana"; one who rides an elephant, signifying great wealth and social status in ancient society</note>. || 1 ||

<vocab>Moonlight of the Ascendant, Human Chart, Lunar Mansion (Nakshatra), Head, Sun Chart, High-ranking Official, Sun, Elephant-rider</vocab>

<summary>This page introduces Chapter Three of the Lagnachandrika, detailing the construction of the 'Nara Chakra' (Human Chart). It explains how to distribute the 27 Nakshatras across the human body starting from the Sun's position to predict a person's fortune based on their birth star's placement.</summary>
<keywords>Nara Chakra, Ravi Chakra, Nakshatras, Vedic astrology, Lagnachandrika, sun chart, astrological anatomy, royal service</keywords>

--- Page 99 ---
<meta>This page continues the interpretation of the Sun Chart (Nara Chakra) from page 94, detailing the specific physical locations of the birth constellation on a human figure. It then transitions into a new predictive tool: the Moon Chart (Chandra Chakra).</meta>

<page-num>95</page-num>
<header>With Vernacular Commentary</header>

If the constellation is on the arms, the person will be strong. If the constellation is on the hands, they may become a thief. If it is on the heart, they are born a lord <note>original: "Ishvara"; a person of great power, wealth, or god-like status</note>. If it is on the navel, they are satisfied with very little. || 2 ||

If the constellation falls on the private parts <note>original: "Guhya"; literally the "hidden" or "secret" parts</note>, they will pursue the wives of others. If on the knees, they will go to foreign lands. If one's own birth constellation <note>the *Janma-Nakshatra*, or the lunar mansion occupied by the moon at birth</note> is situated on the feet, they will be poor and have a short life. || 3 ||

### Now, the Moon Chart

->Calculation of the Moon's Auspicious and Inauspicious Effects<-

The wise astrologer should count from the constellation of the birth-sign <note>the *Janma-Rashi*</note> to the current constellation; then, they should declare the favorable or unfavorable fruits of the Moon. || 1 ||

The best of astrologers should distribute the constellations as follows: six on the face, six on the back, six on the hands, three on the anus <note>original: "Guda"</note>, three on the feet, and three on the throat. || 2 ||

One should know that loss occurs if the stars fall on the face, and loss of wealth occurs if they fall on the back. Fear of the king <note>symbolizing legal troubles or conflict with government authorities</note> is known if they are on the hands, while honor from the king is received if they are on the private parts. || 3 ||

<vocab>Nakshatra (Lunar Mansion), Chandra-chakra (Moon Chart), Jyotishi (Astrologer), Shubhav-ashubha (Auspicious/Inauspicious), Janma-Rashi (Birth-sign)</vocab>

If the constellation falls on the feet, one will be displaced from their position <note>meaning a loss of status or being forced to leave one's home</note>; if on the throat, all will be happy.
