### Now, the Mars Chart

Beginning with the constellation where Mars <note>original: "Bhauma" or "Mangal"</note> is located, place three constellations on the head. Then place three on the eyes, three on the forehead, and four on the pair of arms. || 1 ||

Place three constellations starting from the one Mars occupies on the head, then three on the eyes, three on the forehead <note>original: "Mauli"</note>, and four constellations on both arms. || 1 ||

Place two constellations on the throat, five on the heart, three on the private parts <note>original: "Guhya"; the Sanskrit suggests the secret or hidden regions</note>, and four on the feet <note>original: "Shrutih"; literally 'the Vedas', a traditional Sanskrit poetic code for the number four</note>. If it is on the face, illness occurs; if in the eyes, wealth; if on the forehead, fame; and if on the heart, wealth. || 2 ||

Place two constellations on the throat, five on the heart, three on the anus, and four on the feet. If on the face, there is disease; in the eyes, wealth; on the head, fame; and on the heart, wealth. || 2 ||

If it falls on the throat, one suffers from hiccups; if on the private parts, sexual pleasure <note>the Hindi commentary interprets this as 'aruchi' or loss of appetite/interest, while the Sanskrit 'rati' implies passion or pleasure</note>; if on the feet, one travels to foreign lands. On the left arm, illness occurs, and if on the right arm, one becomes an astrologer <note>term: "Ganaka"; a calculator or professional astrologer</note>. || 3 ||

If it falls on the throat, the hiccup disease; on the anus, the disease of apathy; on the feet, one goes to a foreign land; on the left arm, there is illness; and if on the right arm, one becomes an astrologer. || 3 ||

### Now, the Mercury Chart

Write the constellations in sequence starting from where Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> is positioned. There are five on the face for knowledge, and five on the eyes for kingship <note>symbolizing political power or high governance</note>. || 1 ||

Write them in order starting from the constellation Mercury is on. There are five on the face for knowledge and five on the eyes for the sake of ruling a kingdom. || 1 ||

Write the constellations in order, starting from the one where Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> is located—among them, five constellations placed on the mouth grant knowledge, and five constellations placed on the eyes grant sovereignty. || 1 ||

### Now, the Jupiter Chart
<note>original: "Guru-Chakra"</note>

> Four on the crown grant a kingdom, and a set of four on the pair of shoulders grants wealth; 
> one on the throat brings abundance, and five <note>original: "Madan-hari", a poetic numerical code for five</note> on the chest bring the gain of affection. 
> Six bring pain to the pair of feet, and four <note>original: "Jaladhi", literally 'the oceans', used here to mean four</note> on the left arm bring death; 
> three on the pair of eyes grant happiness equal to a king. This is the Chart of Jupiter <note>original: "Vakpati", meaning 'Lord of Speech', a common title for the planet Jupiter</note>. || 1 ||

Four constellations on the head are grantors of a kingdom. Four constellations on both shoulders grant wealth <note>original: "Lakshmi", the goddess of prosperity</note>. One constellation on the throat grants great power. Five constellations placed on the heart provide affection. Six constellations placed on both feet cause suffering. Then, four constellations located on the left arm bring death. Three constellations on both eyes grant happiness equal to that of a king. This is said to be the auspicious and inauspicious fruit of the Jupiter Chart. || 1 ||

### Now, the Venus Chart
<note>original: "Shukra-chakram"</note>

> Place five constellations on the crown, two on the mouth, and four on the heart, starting from the planet's current position.
> Place seven on the arms, three on the private parts, two on the knees, and four <note>original: "jaladhi", literally 'the oceans', a traditional numerical code for four</note> on the feet. || 1 ||

From the constellation where Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note> is currently located, count and place five constellations on the head, two on the mouth, four constellations on the heart, seven constellations on the arms, three constellations on the anus <note>original: "guda"</note>, then two constellations on the knees, and four constellations on the feet. || 1 ||

> Happiness is found on the heart and the crown; on the private parts, death is certain.
> On the mouth, one receives fine food; on the arms, death; and on the knees and feet, there is pain. || 2 ||

If the birth constellation <note>original: "Janma Nakshatra"</note> falls on the heart or the crown, there will be happiness. If it falls on the private parts, it signifies death. On the mouth, one will receive delicious food. If it falls on the arms, it brings death, and if it falls on the feet, there will be suffering. || 2 ||

### Now, the Saturn Chart
<note>original: "Shani-chakram"</note>

> Whichever constellation Saturn is currently traversing, place that constellation on the mouth.
->Place four on the right hand and six on the pair of feet.<-
> Place four on the left hand, five on the belly,
> three on the head, two on the eyes, and two on the private parts. || 1 ||

Whichever constellation <note>original: "Nakshatra"</note> Saturn is currently in should be placed on the mouth in the "Human Chart" <note>original: "Nara-chakra"</note>. Then, place the next four constellations on the right hand, six constellations on both feet, four constellations on the left hand, five on the belly, three on the head, two on the eyes, and two constellations on the private parts. || 1 ||

> When the Son of the Sun <note>original: "Bhanusute", a title for Saturn</note> is situated on the mouth, there is extreme pain; wealth and fame come when he is in the right hand.
> On the pair of feet, there is failure; and on the left hand, there is doubt regarding the survival of the body in battle. || 2 ||

If Saturn is positioned on the mouth, it causes great suffering. If it is on the right hand, one attains wealth <note>original: "Lakshmi"</note> and fame. On both feet, it brings fruitlessness, and if it is on the left hand, there is a fear of death or physical harm during conflict. || 2 ||

> Wealth is indicated on the heart, sovereignty on the head, supreme happiness on the eyes, and danger to life at the anus; thus should the results of the Saturn Chart be declared. || 3 ||

> It moves from the mouth to the private parts, from the private parts to the head, from the head to the eyes, and from the eyes it goes to the heart. || 4 ||

> From the heart to the left hand, from the left hand to both feet, and from the feet to the right hand; this is called the movement of Saturn. || 5 ||

From the heart it moves to the left hand, from the left hand to both feet, and from the feet it arrives at the right hand. This is known as *Shanishara* <term>Shanishara: literally "Saturn-walking," referring to the transit or progression of the planet through different parts of the human-shaped astrological chart</term> or the movement of Saturn. || 5 ||

### Now, the Rahu Chart

Place two constellations on the heart, one on the mouth, five on the left hand, and assign one constellation to the navel. || 2 ||

### Now, the Ketu Chart
<note>Ketu is the South Node of the moon, representing karmic collections, spirituality, and sudden changes.</note>

One should declare the results starting from the constellation where Ketu is located: two constellations on the eyes bring disease and grief, and five constellations on the mouth bring profit. || 1 ||

<gloss>Tell the result of the constellation on which Ketu is positioned in this way: the first two constellations on the eyes give disease and sorrow, and the five constellations on the arms <note>The Sanskrit "mukhe" means mouth, but the Hindi commentary specifies "bhuja" or arms, a common variation in these diagrams</note> are profit-giving.</gloss> || 1 ||

Three constellations on the crown of the head are described as bestowing a kingdom, and four constellations on the right hand grant fame. || 2 ||

Four on the left hand always cause fear and disease; one on the navel leads to destruction, and two on the private parts cause death. || 3 ||

## Now, the Women’s Chart

One should place three constellations on the crown of the head, seven in the mouth, and eight on the breasts. Place three in the heart, three in the navel, and three in the private parts. || 1 ||

<gloss>Starting from the Sun’s constellation <note>The specific star the sun was passing through at the time of birth.</note>, place three on the head, seven on the mouth, eight on the breasts, three in the heart, three on the navel, and three on the private parts.</gloss> || 1 ||

When on the head, the Sun causes distress; in the mouth, he provides sweet foods; on the breasts, he is said to grant desires; and in the heart, he gives happiness to women. || 2 ||

<gloss>If the birth constellation falls on the head, the Sun causes suffering; on the mouth, he grants delicious food; on the breasts, he grants fulfillment of wishes; and if located on the heart, he brings happiness to the woman.</gloss> || 2 ||

On the navel, he grants happiness to the husband, and on the private parts, he always fulfills desires. This chart, known as the <term>Surya-dimbha</term> <note>original: "Surya-dimbhakhya-chakram". Literally the "Sun-Form" or "Sun-Embryo" chart, a diagnostic tool for women's horoscopes.</note>, is specifically described for women. || 3 ||

<gloss>If it falls on the navel, it grants happiness to the husband, and if on the private parts, it fulfills worldly desires. Thus, this chart called the Surya-dimbha is said to be especially fruitful for women.</gloss> || 3 ||

## Now, the Sun’s Fire-of-Time Chart
<note>original: "Surya-kalanala-chakram". This is a specialized astrological grid used to predict conflict, success, or danger. The name "Kalanala" refers to the "Fire of Time" or the "Fire of Destruction" associated with Lord Shiva.</note>

One should draw three vertical lines with the tips shaped like a <term>trident</term> <note>original: "trishula," the three-pronged spear of the god Shiva.</note>. Draw three horizontal lines across them. Draw two lines at each of the corners, and form a pair of horns with one line. || 1 ||

<gloss>Draw three lines upwards in the shape of trident tips, draw three lines horizontally, draw two lines each at the corners, and create two horns from a single line.</gloss> || 1 ||

Beneath the staff of the central trident, one should write the Sun’s <term>constellation</term> <note>original: "nakshatra". The specific lunar mansion the sun occupied at the time of birth or the time of inquiry.</note>. Then, one should write all the other constellations, along with <term>Abhijit</term> <note>An "intercalary" or extra 28th constellation used in specific calculations, usually omitted in standard 27-star systems.</note>, in order at the tips of these line-staffs. || 2 ||

<gloss>In the middle, beneath the staff of the trident, write the Sun's constellation. Then, place all the other constellations, including Abhijit, in sequence at the heads of these staffs.</gloss> || 2 ||

The three constellations situated at the bottom cause anxiety, death, or imprisonment. In the eight lines, there is profit; and likewise, in the six constellations... || 3 ||

<summary>This page explains how to construct the "Sun's Fire-of-Time Chart" (Suryakalanala Chakra), a trident-based astrological diagram used to determine life outcomes based on the Sun's position among the 28 constellations.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for using the "Sun’s Fire-of-Time Chart" (Surya-Kalanala-Chakra) before introducing the lunar equivalent, the "Moon’s Fire-of-Time Chart" (Chandra-Kalanala-Chakra). The previous page established the layout of the Sun's chart; this page details the specific outcomes of where a birth star (Nakshatra) lands on that grid.</meta>

<page-num>103</page-num>
<header>With English Commentary</header>

If the birth star <note>original: "Nakshatra"—the lunar mansion or star constellation under which a person is born</note> falls within the three stars positioned at the bottom, there will be agitation, fear, and imprisonment. If it falls within the eight lines of the four corners <term>Rekhashtaka: literally "eight lines," referring to the specific geometry of the chart's corners</term>, there will be profit. If it falls within the six stars located on the diagonal lines, profit will also be achieved. || 3 ||

> If the star is in the two horns, illness is destroyed; in the three points of the trident, death is certain. 
> In disputes, strife, war, suffering from illness, or when departing on a journey, 
> this Sun’s Fire-of-Time Chart should be consulted, as stated by the best of astrologers. || 4 ||

If the star falls in the two "horns" <note>likely the top corners or points of the diagram</note>, disease is cured. If it falls on the lines representing the points of the trident <note>original: "Trishulagra"—the tip of Lord Shiva’s trident</note>, death is inevitable. Wise scholars have said that this Sun’s Fire-of-Time Chart <note>original: "Surya Kalanala Chakra"</note> must be considered during legal disputes, family conflicts, war, periods of painful illness, and at the time of departure. || 4 ||

<header>Lagnachandrika <note>The Moonlight of the Ascendant</note></header>

->The wise practitioner should draw the **Moon’s Fire-of-Time Chart** in the shape of the sky <note>original: "Vyomakaram," meaning vast or circular like the firmament</note>. One should place tridents in the four cardinal directions, making them distinct from the center. || 1 ||<-

->Scholars should draw tridents in the four directions of a sky-shaped chart, emerging separately from the center. || 1 ||<-

->The wise one should first write the Moon-star <note>original: "Chandrabham"—the star where the moon was located at birth</note> at the center of the eastern trident. Then, the other stars <note>original: "Nakshatras"—the lunar mansions</note> should be written in sequence, including the star **Abhijit** <note>an "intercalary" or 28th star used in specific astrological calculations</note>. || 2 ||<-

->In the eastern direction, write the Moon-star in the middle of the trident, and then write the other stars in order, including Abhijit. || 2 ||<-

->The auspicious or inauspicious result is known by where the name-star <note>original: "Namabham"—the star corresponding to the first letter of a person's name</note> is located. If it falls within the tridents, it indicates death; if it falls in the outer eight stars, the result is said to be middling. || 3 ||<-

->One should declare the good or bad fortune based on the direction in which the name-star falls. If the star lands in the middle of the tridents, death occurs; if it lands in the outer eight stars, the result is average. || 3 ||<-

->Profit, safety, victory, and wisdom are promised if the star falls within the "inner eight" of the Moon. The wise should understand that results are of two kinds depending on whether the star is in the trident, the center, or the inner core. || 4 ||<-

->If the star falls among the 8 stars within the Moon’s core <term>Garbhashtaka: literally the "eight of the womb," referring to the inner-most ring of the chart</term>, then profit, well-being, victory, and intelligence increase. One should recognize two types of results from stars placed in the middle of the trident and the core. || 4 ||<-

->Profit and loss, happiness and sorrow, victory and defeat—one should always contemplate these using the **Moon’s Fire-of-Time Chart**. || 5 ||<-

->Gain or loss, pleasure or pain, victory or defeat; these should be daily considered through the Moon’s Fire-of-Time Chart. || 5 ||<-

# With Vernacular Commentary

## Now, the Yama’s Fangs Chart <note>Yama is the Hindu god of death; "Damshtra" refers to his tusks or fangs. This chart is used to identify "lethal" astrological positions.</note>

> The wise practitioner should write nine stars <note>original: "Nakshatras"—the 27 lunar mansions of the zodiac</note> going upwards, nine stars positioned transversely, and nine stars going downwards. || 1 ||

Draw nine stars in an upward direction, nine horizontally, and nine in a downward direction. || 1 ||

> When a crossing of lines <note>original: "Vedha"—an astrological term for the intersection or "piercing" of planetary influences</note> is made using four channels, three stars are clearly marked on the left. In this way, a serpent-shaped **Time Chart** <note>original: "Kalachakra"</note> is produced. || 2 ||

A crossing should be made in four channels <note>original: "Nadi"</note>. Then, write three stars starting from the left. In this manner, the serpent-shaped chart and Time Chart are formed. || 2 ||

> The three stars located in the middle of the mouth are known as the **Face of Time** <note>original: "Kalamukha"</note>. The two stars located in the corners are considered the **Fangs of Time** <note>original: "Kaladamshtra"</note>. || 3 ||

Place three stars in the mouth; these should be understood as the Face of Time. Place two stars in the corners; these are called the two Fangs of Time. || 3 ||

> Counting from the star of the current day to the position of the birth star <note>original: "Janma-nakshatra"—the star the moon was in at the moment of one's birth</note>, one should see where it is situated. If the birth star falls in the Mouth or the Fangs, death occurs; if situated elsewhere, it is auspicious. || 4 ||

Count from the star of the day to the birth star. If the birth star lands in the Mouth or the Fangs, death may occur; if it lands anywhere else, consider it a good sign. || 4 ||

> During fever, loss of sight <note>The Hindi commentary interprets this as falling from heights or sustaining injuries</note>, legal disputes, conflict, or war—if a person’s star falls within the Mouth or the Fangs, they face great danger. || 5 ||

In cases of catching a fever, falling from a height, getting injured, legal disputes, or in battle—one whose star lands in the Mouth or Fangs faces great fear. || 5 ||

### Now, the Fruits of Planetary Piercing <note>In astrology, "Vedha" (piercing) occurs when a planet's position "hits" or obstructs a specific point in a chart, influencing the person's fate.</note>

**Sun’s piercing causes mental anguish; the Son of Earth <note>original: "Dharasute"—an epithet for the planet Mars</note> causes loss of wealth. || 1 ||**

In the previously mentioned "Vedha Chart," if the Sun causes a piercing, there is distress in the mind, and if Mars causes a piercing, there is a loss of wealth. || 1 ||

**Saturn causes disease and pain; Rahu and Ketu <note>The North and South Nodes of the Moon, often depicted as a celestial dragon or serpent</note> bring death. Profit occurs during Jupiter's piercing, and the gain of pleasure during Venus’s <note>original: "Bhargave"—an epithet for the planet Venus</note>. || 2 ||**

In Saturn’s piercing, one suffers from disease and agony; if there is a piercing by Rahu or Ketu, death occurs. If the piercing is by Jupiter, there is profit, and if by Venus, one obtains sensual pleasure. || 2 ||

**The gain of a woman occurs in the Moon’s piercing, and happiness from Mercury’s piercing. These are the fruits declared for the piercing of the birth sign. || 3 ||**

If there is a piercing by the Moon, one gains a wife; if by Mercury, there is happiness. These results are stated based on the piercing of the birth sign <note>original: "Janma Rashi"—the zodiac sign the moon occupied at birth</note>. || 3 ||

### Now, the Fortress Chart <note>original: "Durga-Chakra"—a diagram used to predict the fortunes of a fort, city, or village.</note>

**One should draw a chart in the shape of a fortress, consisting of three lines. Place the village star <note>original: "Grama-nakshatra"—the lunar mansion associated with the name of a specific town or village</note> in the Northeast, along with the star Abhijit. || 1 ||**

Write a chart shaped like a fortress <term>Durgakara</term>. Draw three lines (concentric squares) above it, then place the star of the village in the **Northeast** <term>Ishana</term> corner, placing all the stars including <term>Abhijit</term> <note>Abhijit is the "intercalary" 28th lunar mansion used in specific Vedic calculations</note>. || 1 ||

**Place groups of four [stars] in all the corners, and place three stars in each of the middle sections, along with the planets. || 2 ||**

Place four stars in each of the four corners, and in the middle sections, place three stars each, along with the planets. || 2 ||

## The Fortress Chart

->Krittika [space] [space] [space] Ashlesha [space] [space] [space] Magha<-
->Rohini [space] [space] [space] Punarvasu [space] [space] [space] Purva Phalguni<-
->Mrigashira [space] [space] [space] Pushya [space] [space] Uttara Phalguni<-
->Ardra [space] [space] [space] [space] [space] [space] Hasta<-
->Bharani, Ashwini, Revati [space] [space] **Center of the Fort** [space] [space] Vishakha, Swati, Chitra<-
->Uttara Ashadha [space] [space] [space] [space] [space] [space] Purva Ashadha<-
->Purva Bhadrapada [space] [space] [space] Bhadra <note>Uttara Bhadrapada</note> [space] [space] Mula<-
->Shatabhisha [space] [space] [space] Abhijit [space] [space] Jyeshtha<-
->Dhanishtha [space] [space] [space] Shravana [space] [space] Anuradha<-

<note>This diagram, the Durga Chakra, maps the 28 lunar mansions (Nakshatras) around a central point representing a fortress. By tracking which planet is currently transiting the mansions located in the "Center," an astrologer predicts the fort's fate.</note>

> **If the Sun is situated in the middle of the fortress, the drying up of water occurs. If the Moon is there, there is a breach; if Mars, there is burning; if Mercury is in the middle, the King is endowed with wisdom. || 3 ||**

If the Sun enters the center of the fortress, the water (within the fort) will dry up. If the Moon is there, the fort will be breached. If Mars is present, the fort will burn. If Mercury is in the center of the fort, the King acts with great wisdom. || 3 ||

> **If Jupiter is in the middle of the fortress, there will be great abundance. If Venus, the King’s mind becomes unstable; if Saturn, there is division and breach. || 4 ||**
> **If Rahu or Ketu <note>The lunar nodes, regarded as shadow planets</note> come into the middle of the fortress, the King is consumed by poison.**

If Jupiter is in the fort, there is "Subhiksha" <term>Subhiksha: a time of plenty where food and water are easily obtained</term>, meaning grain and water remain plentiful. If Venus is there, the King's mind becomes fickle or wavering. If Saturn is present, the fort will suffer cracks or be broken apart. If Rahu or Ketu enter the center of the fort, that King will be destroyed by poison. || 4 ||

<note>The vocabulary below provides the technical Sanskrit terms used in the verses and diagram.</note>
**Technical Terms:** <term>Durga-chakram</term> (Fortress Chart), <term>Jala-shosha</term> (evaporation/drying of water), <term>Subhiksha</term> (abundance/prosperity), <term>Chalamana</term> (unsteady/fickle), <term>Visha-dagdha</term> (poison-stricken), <term>Nripa</term> (King/Ruler). 
**Lunar Mansions (Nakshatras):** Krittika, Ashlesha, Magha, Rohini, Punarvasu, Purva Phalguni, Mrigashira, Pushya, Uttara Phalguni, Ardra, Hasta, Vishakha, Swati, Shatabhisha, Uttara Ashadha, Purva Ashadha, Mula, Jyeshtha, Dhanishtha, Shravana, Anuradha.

It details how different planets affecting the "center" of the fort can cause outcomes ranging from water shortages and fire to prosperity and wise leadership.</summary>

<header>Moon of the Ascendant</header>

> **If the Sun, Saturn, Mars, Rahu, or Ketu are situated within the fort, the destruction of the fortress occurs. || 5 ||**
> **If Jupiter, Venus, Mercury, or the Moon are situated within the fort, then that fortress cannot be broken, even if attacked by Indra <note>The King of the Gods and wielder of the thunderbolt</note> himself. || 6 ||**

If the cruel planets—the Sun, Saturn, Mars, Rahu, or Ketu <note>The north and south nodes of the moon, respectively</note>—are positioned in the "middle" mansions of the fortress chart, the fort will be breached and the citadel destroyed. However, if the benefic planets—Jupiter, Venus, Mercury, and the Moon—enter the center of the fort, that fortress will remain invincible, even if the great god Indra attempts to break it. || 5-6 ||

### Regarding the Average Transit of the Sun and Others

> **Venus, Mercury, and the Sun [stay in a sign] for one month; the Moon for two and a quarter days. Mars stays for one and a half months <note>Original: *tri-pakṣam*, meaning three fortnights</note>, Jupiter for a year, and Saturn for two and a half years. ||**
> **Rahu and Ketu always consume [one sign] for one and a half years. || 1 ||**

Venus, Mercury, and the Sun stay in each zodiac sign for approximately one month. The Moon always stays for two and a quarter (2<gloss>1/4</gloss>) days. Mars stays for one and a half months, Jupiter for one year, Saturn for two and a half years, and Rahu and Ketu stay in a sign for one and a half years. || 1 ||

### Regarding the Knowledge of the Birth Ascendant

> **If the Moon does not aspect the Ascendant, and the Lord of the Ascendant does not aspect it, and if the Sun also does not aspect the Ascendant, he is then said to be "born of another." || 1 ||**

If the Moon does not "look at" <note>An "aspect" is a geometric relationship between planets/points</note> the Ascendant (the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth), and the planet that rules that Ascendant also does not aspect it, and the Sun also fails to aspect the Ascendant, then that child should be understood to be fathered by another <note>This is a traditional rule regarding legitimacy/biological fatherhood in ancient horoscopy</note>. || 1 ||

> **If the Lord of the House of Children is set <note>Combust, or too close to the sun to be seen</note>, conjoined with cruel planets, or placed between cruel planets, or if the Moon is conjoined with cruel planets in a quadrant, it causes obstacles to offspring. || 2 ||**

<note>Definitions of technical terms:</note>
**Fortress:** <term>Durga</term> (A fortified city or castle)
**Planet:** <term>Graha</term> (The celestial influencers)
**Average Transit:** <term>Madhyamacara</term> (The mean motion of a planet through the zodiac)
**Ascendant:** <term>Lagna</term> (The rising sign at the time of birth)
**Offspring:** <term>Santati</term> (Children or lineage)
**Cruel Planet:** <term>Papagraha</term> (Malefics, such as Mars or Saturn)
**Plus a quarter:** <term>Sapada</term> (1/4)
**Plus a half:** <term>Sardha</term> (1/2)

<page-num>१०१</page-num>
<header>With Language Commentary</header>

If the lord of the fifth house <note>the house governing children and creativity</note> is combust <note>rendered invisible by its proximity to the Sun</note>, or is associated with malefic planets, or is positioned between two malefic planets, and the Moon is also joined with a malefic planet, then that individual will face obstacles regarding offspring. || 2 ||

->Starting from sunrise, take the elapsed units of time and halve that number.<-
->Count that number from the Sun’s constellation to determine the Ascendant. || 3 ||<-

Starting from sunrise, take the number of elapsed *ghatis* <note>a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>—this is called the *Ishta*—and divide it by two. Count that resulting number starting from the Sun’s constellation <term>Nakshatra|One of the 27 lunar mansions or star clusters</term>. The constellation you arrive at should be understood as the constellation of the Ascendant <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note>. (This is considered a general or approximate method). || 3 ||

->Three women for Pisces and Aries; four for Taurus and Aquarius.<-
->Five for Gemini and Capricorn; and five for Sagittarius and Cancer. || 4 ||<-

If the Ascendant is Pisces or Aries, there are three women present [at the time of birth]. In Taurus or Aquarius, there are four women. In Gemini or Capricorn, there are five women, and in Sagittarius or Cancer, there are also five women. || 4 ||

->In the birthing chamber, five women are known for Virgo and Libra.<-
->For other Ascendants, the wise say there are three women. || 5 ||<-

When the birth occurs under the signs of Virgo or Libra, there are five women near the woman in labor. If the birth occurs under any other sign [not previously mentioned], the scholars state there are only three women. || 5 ||

->As many planets as are in the Ascendant or near its lord,<-
->Or in the second and twelfth houses—that is how many women are remembered. || 6 ||<-

Alternatively, one may count the number of planets situated in the Ascendant, or those positioned next to the Lord of the Ascendant, or those in the second house <note>the house of wealth/sustenance</note> or the twelfth house <note>the house of loss/seclusion</note>. That total number indicates how many women are near the mother at the time of delivery. || 6 ||

->Or, as many planets as are between the Moon and the Ascendant.<-
->Rahu represents the bed, while Mars is the breaker of the site. || 7 ||<-

Furthermore, the number of women corresponds to the number of planets situated between the Moon and the Ascendant. In this context, Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> describes the condition of the birthing bed, while Mars acts as the "breaker of the site" <note>original: *kshetra-bhanga-da*, suggesting a rupture or physical complication in the birthing area</note>.

<note>Vocabulary: Offspring (Santan), Malefic planet (Papagraha), Moon (Chandrama), Constellation (Nakshatra), Ascendant (Lagna), Woman in childbirth (Sutika), North Node (Rahu), Mars (Mangal), Breaker of the site (Kshetrabhangada).</note>

<header>Moonlight of the Ascendant</header>

One should mention a woman [attending the birth]; in whichever direction Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> is located, that is the direction where the birthing bed was placed. In whichever house Mars is situated, that indicates the place where the umbilical cord was severed. || 7 ||

->Where the Sun is positioned, there is a lamp; where Saturn is, iron is present.<-
->Through three cycles of the twelve zodiac signs beginning with Aries,<-
->the entrance of the house, starting from the East, is determined from the time of birth. || 8 ||<-

One should state that a lamp <note>original: "deepah"</note> was located in the position of the Sun and iron was present in the position of Saturn. By calculating three cycles of the twelve zodiac signs—starting from Aries—based on the Ascendant, one can determine whether the house's entrance faced East or another direction based on the time of birth. || 8 ||

### Now, the Sequence of the Ashtottari Dasha

->Assign four constellations to the malefic planets and three to the benefic planets.<-
->One should list them from Ardra to Mrigashira, including the constellation Abhijit. || 1 ||<-

One should assign four <term>Nakshatras|Lunar mansions or star clusters</term> to the malefic planets <note>such as the Sun, Mars, Saturn, and Rahu</note> and three constellations to the benefic planets <note>such as the Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus</note>. Arrange all the constellations starting from Ardra and ending with Mrigashira, including <term>Abhijit|An intercalary or "extra" constellation used in specific Vedic calculations</term>. || 1 ||

->The Sun's period is six years; the Moon's is exactly fifteen.<-
->For Mars, it is eight years; for Mercury, it is seventeen. || 2 ||<-
->For Saturn, it is ten years; for Jupiter, nineteen.<-
->For Rahu, twelve years; and for Venus, it is twenty-one. || 3 ||<-

In the <term>Ashtottari Dasha|A planetary cycle system based on a total span of 108 years</term>, the period of the Sun lasts for 6 years, the Moon for 15 years, Mars for 8 years, Mercury for 17 years, Saturn for 10 years, Jupiter for 19 years, Rahu for 12 years, and the period of Venus is 21 years. (The total sum of the years for all these periods is 108). || 2-3 ||

->Multiply the elapsed units of time by the measure of the maximum lifespan.<-
->Divide by the portion of the constellation; then refine the result by ninety. || 4 ||<-

Multiply the elapsed *nadis* <note>traditional units of time; 1 nadi is approximately 24 minutes</note> by the measure of the maximum lifespan <note>108 years</note>. Then, divide that by the total duration of the constellation. Multiply the remainder by 90 and divide once more. This is the procedure for calculating the "elapsed" and "remaining" portions <note>original: "bhukta-bhogya"</note> of the current planetary period. || 4 ||

<vocab>Jyotish, Lagnachandrika, Ashtottari Dasha, Rahu, Mars, Sun, Saturn, Nakshatra, Abhijit, House Entrance, Sequence of Periods</vocab>

<summary>This page details how to reconstruct the physical setting of a birth (bed position, house entrance) using planetary positions and introduces the Ashtottari Dasha, an 108-year cycle of planetary influences.</summary>
<keywords>Vedic astrology, natal conditions, birth room, Ashtottari Dasha, planetary periods, 108-year cycle, Nakshatras</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the Ashtottari Dasha system introduced on page 110. It transitions from calculating the sequence of planetary periods based on birth constellations to the mathematical formula for sub-periods, and begins the specific prognostications for the Sun's major period.</meta>

<page-num>111</page-num>
<header>With the Vernacular Commentary</header>

->The Sun is known to rule four constellations starting from Ardra; the Moon rules three from Magha.<-
->Mars rules four from Hasta; Mercury rules three from Anuradha. || 5 ||<-
->Saturn rules four from Purva Ashadha; Jupiter rules three from Dhanishtha.<-
->Rahu rules four from Uttara Bhadrapada; Venus rules three from Krittika. || 6 ||<-

If a person is born in one of the four <term>Nakshatras|Lunar mansions or star clusters</term> starting from Ardra, they begin under the <term>Dasha|Major planetary period</term> of the <term>Sun</term>. From Magha for three constellations, it is the <term>Moon’s</term> period; from Hasta for four constellations, it is Mars; from Anuradha for three constellations, it is <term>Mercury</term>; from Purva Ashadha <note>The Sanskrit verse uses "Pusha" as a shorthand or variant, but the commentary clarifies the calculation</note> for four constellations, it is <term>Jupiter</term>; from Uttara Bhadrapada for four constellations, it is <term>Rahu</term>; and from Krittika for three constellations, it is the period of <term>Venus</term>. || 5-6 ||

->Multiply the period by its own years and divide that number by nine.<-
->This determines the sub-period; the first sub-period belongs to the ruler of the major period. || 7 ||<-

To calculate the <term>Antardasha|Sub-period</term> of a planet, take the number of years assigned to that planet’s major period and multiply it by that same number. Divide the total by 9; the resulting quotient indicates the months and other units of the sub-period. At the start of any major planetary period, the first sub-period is always that of the ruling planet itself. || 7 ||

# Results of the Sun’s Period

->An anxious heart and suffering for one's kin,<-
->A diseased body and separation from family.<-
->Oppressed by the King’s men and living as a traveler,<-
->A man is harmed by horses during the Sun’s period. || 1 ||<-

In the major period of the Sun, the mind remains restless and anxious. One experiences grief concerning their relatives, illness in the body, and separation from loved ones. One is harassed by <term>Rajadutas|Government officials or royal messengers</term>, lives in foreign lands <note>original: "pravasi" – one who resides away from home</note>, and may suffer an injury or blow involving horses. || 1 ||

<vocab>Nakshatra, Dasha, Antardasha, Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Rahu, Venus, Astrology, Predictive results</vocab>

<header>Lagna Chandrika</header>

## Lagna Chandrika

->In the Sun's sub-period within the Sun's major period, there is gain from the royal court;<-
->Mental distress, the loss of wealth, and separation from kinsmen also occur. || 2 ||<-

If within the <term>Dasha|Major planetary period</term> of the Sun, the <term>Antardasha|Sub-period</term> of the Sun itself occurs <note>The duration is calculated as 4 months</note>, there will be gains, mental anguish, the spending of money, and separation from one's relatives. || 2 ||

->The destruction of enemies and gain of wealth, the end of worries and the arrival of happiness;<-
->When the Moon is in the sub-period of the Sun, the destruction of disease occurs. || 3 ||<-

If within the Sun's major period there is the <term>Moon|Chandra</term> sub-period (duration: 10 months), then enemies are defeated, wealth is acquired, anxieties vanish, and illnesses are cured. || 3 ||

->Gems, pearls, and gold; victory in battle and happiness as well;<-
->One receives honor from the king when Mars is in the sub-period of the Sun. || 4 ||<-

If within the Sun's major period there is the <term>Mars|Mangal</term> sub-period (duration: 5 months and 12 days), one obtains jewels, gold, victory in conflict, and respect from sovereign rulers. || 4 ||

->Pleasure, luxury, and poverty; the onset of disease;<-
->Scabies, eczema, and other such ailments occur when Mercury is in the sub-period of the Sun. || 5 ||<-

If within the Sun's major period there is the <term>Mercury|Budha</term> sub-period (duration: 9 months and 18 days), there is the enjoyment of luxury, the beginning of illness, and skin diseases like <term>Paama|Scabies</term> and <term>Vicharchika|Eczema</term>. || 5 ||

->Fear of the king, fear of enemies, quarrels, and misery;<-
->The destruction of wealth occurs when Saturn is in the sub-period of the Sun. || 6 ||<-

If within the Sun's major period there is the <term>Saturn|Shani</term> sub-period (duration: 6 months and 12 days), one faces fear from the government, fear of enemies, conflict, sorrow, and the loss of financial assets. || 6 ||

->Sinless, free from vices, healthy, and wealthy;<-
->One attains a high status when Jupiter is in the sub-period of the Sun. || 7 ||<-

<note>The text for verse 7 provides a highly positive outlook for the Sun-Jupiter period, focusing on moral character and social advancement, though the vernacular commentary for this verse is incomplete on this page.</note>

### Now, the Results of the Major Period of Mars

->Injury from weapons and suffering caused by the sovereign;<-
->Thieves, fire, disease, and the loss of wealth;<-
->Failure in one's work and the onset of poverty<-
->Occur during the major period of the Son of Earth. || 1 ||<-

<note>original: "Dharanī-suta" (Son of the Earth), a traditional epithet for the planet Mars.</note> If it is the major period of <term>Mangala|Mars</term>, one may experience injuries from weapons, trouble from the government or king, suffering from thieves, fire, or illness, loss of money, the failure of planned tasks, and a state of misery. || 1 ||

->Clashes with enemies and conflict with relatives;<-
->Association with women and fear of blood-bile disorders when Mars enters its own period. || 2 ||<-

<margin>7|3|20|0</margin> If the sub-period of Mars occurs within the major period of Mars (duration: 7 years, 3 months, and 20 days), there will be confrontations with enemies, disputes with kinsmen, association with women, and fear arising from <term>Rakta-Pitta|A medical condition involving internal heat and blood disorders</term>. || 2 ||

->**Great fear arises from enemies, thieves, and the sovereign;**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Budha|Mercury</term> (duration: 1 year, 5 months, 3 days, 20 hours) occurs during the major period of <term>Mangala|Mars</term>, there will be specific fear regarding enemies, thieves, and the government or king. || 3 ||

---

->**Loss of wealth and great sorrow occur here incessantly;**<-
->**A person always faces calamities when Saturn enters the period of Mars. || 4 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Shani|Saturn</term> <note>original: "Manda," an epithet for Saturn meaning 'the slow-moving one'.</note> (duration: 8 months, 26 days, 40 hours) occurs during the major period of Mars, there will be destruction, constant profound sorrow, and the person will perpetually encounter misfortunes. || 4 ||

---

->**Gain of wealth, gain from pilgrimages, and the worship of gods and Brahmins;**<-
->**One should predict some fear from the sovereign when Jupiter enters the period of Mars. || 5 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Brihaspati|Jupiter</term> <note>original: "Jiva," meaning the 'living force'.</note> (duration: 16 months, 26 days, 40 hours) occurs during the major period of Mars, one will attain wealth and the benefits of visiting holy places, become a worshiper of deities and the learned, and—though some say otherwise—may experience a slight fear of the king. || 5 ||

---

->**Fear of weapons, thieves, and fire; suffering regarding agriculture, women, and wealth;**<-
->**One should predict fear from various sources when Rahu enters the period of Mars. || 6 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Rahu|the North Node</term> (duration: 10 months, 20 days, 10 hours) occurs during the major period of Mars, there will be danger from weapons, thieves, and fire, as well as hardships involving farming, one's wife, or finances, with fear arising from many directions. || 6 ||

---

->**Diseases, fear of enemies, loss of wealth, and disturbances;**<-
->**Men travel to foreign lands when Venus enters the period of Mars. || 7 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Shukra|Venus</term> <note>original: "Sita," meaning 'the bright one'.</note> (duration: 28 months, 20 days) occurs during the major period of Mars, it results in illness, fear of foes, financial ruin, social unrest, and travel to distant or foreign countries. || 7 ||

---

->**Health, auspiciousness in all directions, and celebrations of victory in the royal court;**<-
->**Acquisition of wealth occurs here when the Sun enters the period of Mars. || 8 ||**<-

<note>This final verse predicts that the sub-period of the Sun within Mars brings vitality, success in legal or political matters, and financial prosperity.</note>

# With Commentary in the Common Tongue

If the sub-period of the <term>Surya|Sun</term> (duration: 5 months, 10 days) occurs during the major period of <term>Mangala|Mars</term>, there will be good health, overall well-being, victory in matters concerning the king or government, as well as celebrations and the acquisition of wealth. || 8 ||

## Now, the Results of the Period of Mercury

->**Endowed with hundreds of various kinds of wealth,**<-
->**Engaged in play with beautiful women and living luxuriously;**<-
->**A person attains success in all goals and is highly honored,**<-
->**During the major period of Mercury. || 1 ||**<-

If the major period of <term>Budha|Mercury</term> is active, the person is endowed with hundreds of different types of assets, consorts with beautiful women, enjoys a luxurious lifestyle, achieves success in all their aims, and becomes highly venerable. || 1 ||

->**Devotion to intelligence and righteousness, meeting friends and kin;**<-
->**The rise of enemies and physical suffering when Mercury is within Mercury. || 2 ||**<-

If the sub-period of Mercury (duration: 32 months, 3 days, 2 hours) occurs within the major period of Mercury itself, there will be the development of intelligence, a love for <term>Dharma|righteousness</term>, reunions with friends and relatives, but also the emergence of enemies and bodily pain. || 2 ||

->**Sudden meeting with enemies, sudden collection of wealth;**<-
->**Contact with fire, poison, and such when Saturn enters Mercury. || 3 ||**<-

<note>The text concludes here mid-sentence regarding the sub-period of Saturn within the major period of Mercury, noting unexpected encounters and dangers from fire or toxins.</note>

<header>Lagnachandrika (Moonlight of the Ascendant)</header>

# Lagnachandrika

If the <term>Antardasha|sub-period</term> of <term>Shani|Saturn</term> (duration: 18 months, 26 days, 40 <term>ghatis|a traditional unit of time, roughly 24 minutes</term>) occurs during the major period of <term>Budha|Mercury</term>, there will be sudden encounters with enemies and sudden collection of wealth, as well as contact with poison and fire. || 3 ||

->**Gaining gold and other metals, as well as bodily health;**<-
->**The acquisition of property and virtue when Jupiter is within Mercury. || 4 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Brihaspati|Jupiter</term> (duration: 36 months, 26 days, 40 ghatis) occurs during the major period of Mercury, there will be gains of gold and other metals, bodily health, and the acquisition of property and wealth. || 4 ||

->**Intense enthusiasm and strength, effort in various tasks and war;**<-
->**Endowed with wealth, virtue, and enjoyment when Rahu is within Mercury. || 5 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Rahu|the North Node of the Moon</term> (duration: 22 months, 20 days) occurs during the major period of Mercury, the person will possess great valor, engage in many tasks and efforts in battle, and be endowed with wealth and religious merit. || 5 ||

->**Love for worshiping the Teacher and the Gods, as well as a fondness for knowledge and virtue;**<-
->**Equipped with fine clothing and ornaments when Venus is within Mercury. || 6 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Shukra|Venus</term> (duration: 39 months, 20 days) occurs during the major period of Mercury, there will be a love for the worship of the guru and the deities, an affinity for knowledge and virtue, and the person will be adorned with fine clothes and jewelry. || 6 ||

->**Free from disease, enemies, and fear; the arrival of sons, virtue, and wealth;**<-
->**One becomes respected by kings when the Sun is within Mercury. || 7 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Surya|the Sun</term> (duration: 11 months, 1 day) occurs during the major period of Mercury, one is freed from illness, enemies, and fear. Sons, virtue, and wealth will arrive, and the person will be honored by kings. || 7 ||

->**Wasting disease and leprosy, various pains in the body;**<-
->**Diseases of the throat also arise when the Moon is within Mercury. || 8 ||**<-
<note>This final verse describes the physical afflictions associated with the sub-period of the Moon during the major period of Mercury.</note>

If the <term>Antardasha|sub-period</term> of <term>Mangal|Mars</term> (duration: 8 months, 26 days, 40 ghatis, 0 palas <note>Ghatis and Palas are traditional Indian units of time; a ghati is 24 minutes.</note>) occurs during the major period of <term>Shani|Saturn</term>, there will be exile from one's country, loss of wealth, the arrival of enemies and diseases, and great fear. || 8 ||

### Now, the Results of the Major Period of Jupiter

> One is fulfilled here by <term>Dharma, Artha, and Kama|the three aims of life: righteousness, prosperity, and pleasure</term>, joined with royal glory and humility;
> During the period of <term>Guru|Jupiter</term>, a man is wealthy, victorious, joined with a wife and sons, and free from disease. || 1 ||

In the major period of <term>Brihaspati|Jupiter</term>, a person becomes full of righteousness, wealth, and worldly desires; they possess royal status, majesty, and a humble nature, and they enjoy a body free from disease. || 1 ||

> The birth of a son, the gain of wealth, and the gathering of all kinds of gems;
> The gain of jewels occurs when Jupiter enters within Jupiter. || 2 ||

If the sub-period of Jupiter (duration: 4 months, 3 days, 20 ghatis, 0 palas) occurs during the major period of Jupiter, there will be the birth of a son, the acquisition of wealth, the collection of all types of precious stones, and the profit of jewels. || 2 ||

> Delusion from eruptions such as smallpox, grief, disease, and loss of wealth;
> When Rahu enters within Jupiter, there will be fear of enemies. || 3 ||

If the sub-period of <term>Rahu|the North Node</term> (duration: 2 months, 5 days, 10 ghatis, 0 palas) occurs during the major period of Jupiter, there will be diseases such as <term>Vishphota|eruptive boils</term> (such as <term>Shitala|smallpox</term>), as well as delusion, sorrow, illness, the destruction of wealth, and fear from enemies. || 3 ||

<summary>This page concludes the sub-periods of Saturn, noting the success brought by Mercury, and begins the major period of Jupiter, which generally brings prosperity and health, though the sub-period of Rahu introduces disease and loss.</summary>
<keywords>Saturn, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Rahu, Antardasha, smallpox, prosperity, Vedic astrology</keywords>

> Quarrels, mental agony, loss of wealth, and great fear;
> Separation from one's wife occurs when Venus enters within Jupiter. || 4 ||

If the <term>Antardasha|sub-period</term> of <term>Shukra|Venus</term> (duration: 2 years, 8 months <note>original: "मान-मासादि २। ८। ०। ०"</note>) occurs during the major period of <term>Guru|Jupiter</term>, there will be conflict, mental anxiety, destruction of wealth, great fear, and separation from one's wife. || 4 ||

> The destruction of enemies, constant victory, honor from kings, and great happiness;
> Association with powerful people occurs when the Sun enters within Jupiter. || 5 ||

If the sub-period of <term>Surya|the Sun</term> (duration: 9 months, 18 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि ०। ९। १८। ०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Jupiter, there will be the destruction of enemies, daily victory, respect among kings, great happiness, and association with fierce or powerful men. || 5 ||

> Association with many women, becoming physically weakened, yet free from the pain of enemies;
> The birth of a daughter occurs when the Moon enters within Jupiter. || 6 ||

If the sub-period of <term>Chandra|the Moon</term> (duration: 1 year, 4 months <note>original: "मान-मासादि १। ४। ०। ०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Jupiter, one becomes physically depleted due to the company of many women, remains free from the affliction of enemies, and a daughter is born. || 6 ||

> The destruction of foes, acquisition of wealth, and the successful completion of all undertakings;
> Happiness, good fortune, and health occur when Mars enters within Jupiter. || 7 ||

If the sub-period of <term>Mangal|Mars</term> (duration: 11 months, 6 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि ०। ११। ६। ०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Jupiter, there will be the destruction of enemies, gain of wealth, the successful gathering of all tasks, happiness, good fortune, and freedom from disease. || 7 ||

> Skill in intellect and specialized knowledge, the gathering of wealth and kinsmen;
> Devotion to teachers, gods, and the sacrificial fire occurs when Mercury enters within Jupiter. || 8 ||

If the sub-period of <term>Budha|Mercury</term> (duration: 2 years, 3 months, 6 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि २। ३। ६। ०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Jupiter, the person becomes proficient in intellect and science, experiences a gathering of wealth and relatives, and develops devotion toward the <term>Guru|teacher</term>, the deities, and the <term>Agni|sacred fire</term>. || 8 ||

## Now, the Results of the Major Period of Rahu

> Loss of knowledge, traveling to foreign lands,
> The decline of righteousness and various diseases;
> Emptiness everywhere and danger to the body
> Are certain for a man during the major period of Rahu. || 1 ||

In the major period of <term>Rahu|the North Node of the Moon</term> <note>Rahu is a "shadow planet" in Vedic astrology, often associated with sudden changes and foreign influences</note>, there is a loss of wisdom, travel to foreign countries, a decline in religious practice, many types of illnesses, a sense of failure in all activities, and even a threat to one's life. || 1 ||

> Association with the best of the twice-born, gain of a wife, and accumulation of wealth;
> But quarrels with relatives occur when Rahu is within Rahu. || 2 ||

If the sub-period of Rahu (duration: 1 year, 6 months <note>original: "मान-मासादि १।६।०।०।०"</note>) occurs within the major period of Rahu itself, the individual will associate with <term>Brahmins|priests or scholars</term>, gain a wife, accumulate wealth, but will also experience conflict with their own family members. || 2 ||

> Devoted to righteousness, speaking the truth, wealthy and free from disease;
> One becomes respected by the king when Venus enters within Rahu. || 3 ||

If the sub-period of <term>Shukra|Venus</term> (duration: 2 years, 8 months <note>original: "मान-मासादि २।८।०।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Rahu, the person becomes virtuous, truthful, wealthy, healthy, and receives honors from the ruler. || 3 ||

> Grief regarding sons, great fear, loss of wealth, and deep worry;
> Fear of fire and thieves occurs somewhere when the Sun enters within Rahu. || 4 ||

If the <term>Antardasha|sub-period</term> of <term>Surya|the Sun</term> (duration: 8 months <note>original: "मान-मासादि ८।०।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of <term>Rahu</term>, there will be sorrow concerning one's children, extreme fear, loss of wealth, excessive anxiety, and in some places, the threat of fire and thieves. || 4 ||

> Destruction of wife and wealth, and quarrels with relatives;
> Great fear arises when the Moon enters within Rahu. || 5 ||

> There is even doubt regarding life when Mars enters within Rahu. || 6 ||

> Union with friends and relatives, and the coming together of wealth and grain;
> No suffering of any kind arises when Mercury enters within Rahu. || 7 ||

If the sub-period of <term>Budha|Mercury</term> (duration: 2 years, 2 months, and 2 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि २।२।२।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Rahu, there will be a joyful reunion with friends and family, the arrival of wealth and food in every way, and no sorrow shall occur. || 7 ||

> Abandonment of one's own country, union with family members;
> Loss of servants and wealth when Saturn enters within Rahu. || 8 ||

If the sub-period of <term>Shani|Saturn</term> (duration: 1 year, 3 months, and 10 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि १।३।१०।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Rahu, the individual may leave their own country, live closely with family members, and suffer the loss of both servants and wealth. || 8 ||

# With Vernacular Commentary

**Freedom from disease, constant happiness, the worship of gods and Brahmins,**
**And the prosperity of wealth and grain occurs when Jupiter enters within Rahu. || 9 ||**

<margin>25 months, 1 day <note>original: "२५।१।ग०।" referring to the duration of the sub-period</note></margin> If the <term>Antardasha|sub-period</term> of <term>Brihaspati|Jupiter</term> (duration: 25 months and 1 day <note>original: "मान-मासादि २५।१।ग०"</note>) occurs during the major period of <term>Rahu</term>, the individual will experience a decrease in illness, constant happiness, will become a worshiper of the gods and the learned <term>Brahmins|the priestly/scholarly class</term>, and will possess an abundance of wealth and grain. || 9 ||

---

->Now, the Results of the Period of Venus<-

**Respected by the greatest of kings, filled with the profit of wealth,**
**Possessing elephants and horses, devoted to women,**
**Skilled in the application of mantras and in the scriptures—**
**A man is prosperous and successful during the period of the Poet. || 1 ||**

In the <term>Dasha|major period</term> of <term>Shukra|Venus</term>, a person becomes respected by rulers, is full of financial gains, possesses transport such as elephants and horses, is affectionate toward women, and becomes expert in the use of <term>mantras|sacred chants</term> and the <term>shastras|scriptural sciences</term>, remaining healthy and capable. || 1 || <note>Venus is referred to here as "Kavi," meaning the Poet or the Sage.</note>

**An increase in honor, the birth of a son, the arrival of wealth and grain, happiness,**
**And the gain of gold, garments, and the like occurs when the White One enters within the White One. || 2 ||**

<margin>49 months <note>original: "४९।ग०।"</note></margin> If the sub-period of <term>Shukra|Venus</term> (duration: 49 months <note>original: "मान-मासादि ४९।ग०"</note>) occurs within the major period of Venus itself, there will be an increase in prestige, the birth of a son, the coming of wealth and grain, general happiness, and the acquisition of gold and fine clothing. || 2 || <note>The text uses "Sita" (the White One), a traditional name for the planet Venus.</note>

**The destruction of enemies, constant victory, profit from the king, and great happiness,**
**And association with powerful people occurs when the Sun enters within Venus. || 3 ||**

<margin>14 months, 8 days <note>original: "१४।८।ग०।"</note></margin> If the sub-period of <term>Surya|the Sun</term> (duration: 14 months and 8 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि १४।८।ग०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Venus, it results in the destruction of enemies, constant victory, benefits received from rulers, great happiness, and companionship with <term>prachanda|powerful or fierce</term> individuals. || 3 ||

**Devotion to the Guru, the gods, and the sacred fire; moderate pain and happiness;**
**And the meeting of both enemies and friends occurs when the Moon enters within Venus. || 4 ||**

If the <term>Antardasha|sub-period</term> of the <term>Chandra|Moon</term> (duration: 3 months and 10 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि ३।१०।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of <term>Shukra|Venus</term>, there will be devotion toward the teacher (Guru), the deities, and the ritual fire. The individual will experience a mixture of suffering and joy, and will encounter both enemies and friends. || 4 ||

**Having conquered the enemy in battle, wealth and fame are attained;**
**Health, happiness, and prosperity occur when Mars enters within Venus. || 5 ||**

If the sub-period of <term>Mangala|Mars</term> (duration: 2 months, 8 days, and 20 hours <note>original: "मान-मासादि २।८।२०।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Venus, the individual will gain wealth and renown by defeating enemies in conflict. They will also attain physical health, happiness, and worldly power. || 5 ||

**Diseases of the nails and the head, and suffering arising from the stomach;**
**Pain is produced in the body when Mercury enters within Venus. || 6 ||**

If the sub-period of <term>Budha|Mercury</term> (duration: 3 months, 9 days, and 20 hours <note>original: "मान-मासादि ३।९।२०।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Venus, there will be ailments affecting the fingernails and the head, distress originating in the <term>amashaya|stomach or abdomen</term>, and general bodily pain. || 6 ||

**Association with wicked women, happiness, and the arrival of wealth;**
**The destruction of enemies and the gain of friends occurs when Saturn enters within Venus. || 7 ||**

If the sub-period of <term>Shani|Saturn</term> (duration: 2 months, 3 days, and 10 hours <note>original: "मान-मासादि २।३।१०।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Venus, the person may associate with ill-intentioned women, but will also experience happiness, the accumulation of wealth, the vanishing of enemies, and the acquisition of good friends. || 7 ||

**Prosperity of wealth and grain, endowed with various religious duties;**
**One attains leadership over the guilds when Jupiter enters within Venus. || 8 ||**

If the sub-period of <term>Brihaspati|Jupiter</term> (duration: 4 months, 4 days, and 10 hours <note>original: "मान-मासादि ४।४।१०।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Venus, there will be an abundance of money and food. The individual will be involved in various righteous or religious acts and will become a leader or master over many people <note>The term "shreni-prabhuttva" suggests becoming the head of a professional guild or social group.</note>. || 8 ||

### Now, the Results of the Vimshottari Dasha
<note>The Vimshottari Dasha is a 120-year cycle of planetary influences based on the position of the Moon at birth. It is the primary timing system used by astrologers to predict when specific life events will occur.</note>

**Six years for the Sun and ten for the Moon; seven years for Mars;**
**Eighteen years for Rahu and sixteen for Jupiter. || 1 ||**
**Nineteen for Saturn and seventeen for Mercury;**
**Seven years for Ketu and likewise twenty for Venus. || 2 ||**

The duration of the <term>Vimshottari Dasha|120-year planetary cycle</term> is assigned as follows: the Sun for 6 years, the Moon for 10 years, Mars for 7 years, Rahu for 18 years, Jupiter for 16 years, Saturn for 19 years, Mercury for 17 years, Ketu for 7 years, and Venus for 20 years. Understand the system in this manner. || 1-2 ||

**Starting the count from the Krittika star and ending with Bharani;**
**Repeating the cycle from Krittika three times, one should calculate the planets starting from the Sun in their proper order. || 3 ||**

One should count the <term>Nakshatras|lunar mansions</term> from <term>Krittika</term> through to <term>Bharani</term>. Starting from Krittika, the periods of the Sun and the other planets occur in three repeating cycles. For example, if a person is born when the Moon is in the Krittika constellation, their life begins under the major period of the Sun; if the birth constellation is Rohini, it is the period of the Moon. All other periods should be understood in this same sequence. || 3 ||
<note>The 27 Nakshatras are divided into three groups of nine, each group being ruled by the same sequence of nine "planets": Sun, Moon, Mars, Rahu, Jupiter, Saturn, Mercury, Ketu, and Venus.</note>

---

### Now, the Results of the Ketu Dasha

->The destruction of wealth and calamity for the wife;<-
->Bodily suffering and the loss of honor from the king.<-

<header>The Moonlight of the Ascendant</header>

->**Separation from loved ones and family members will occur;**<-
->**And in the period of Ketu, there will be constant distress. || 1 ||**<-

During the <term>Dasha|major period</term> of <term>Ketu|the South Node of the Moon</term>, there will be a loss of wealth, calamities befalling one's wife, physical pain, loss of honor among rulers, separation from one's beloved friends and family members, and constant mental or physical suffering. || 1 ||

---

->**Loss of sons, loss of wealth, and quarrels with wicked women;**<-
->**When Ketu is within Ketu, there is fear of rulers and conflict with enemies. || 2 ||**<-

If the <term>Antardasha|sub-period</term> of Ketu itself (duration: 4 months and 27 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि ४।२७।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Ketu, there will be the loss of a son, disputes with malicious women, fear of government authorities, and conflict or war with enemies. || 2 ||

---

->**Abandonment of the wife, burning by fire, the birth of a daughter, and fever;**<-
->**When Venus is within Ketu, there will be quarrels with friends. || 3 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Shukra|Venus</term> (duration: 1 year and 2 months <note>original: "मान-मासादि १४।०।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Ketu, one may experience the separation from or abandonment of a wife, injuries from fire, the birth of a daughter, fever, and quarrels with friends. || 3 ||

---

->**Burning by fire, fever, disease, and traveling to foreign lands;**<-
->**When the Sun is within Ketu, wasting diseases also arise. || 4 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Surya|the Sun</term> (duration: 4 months and 6 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि ४।६।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Ketu, there will be injuries from fire, fever, general illness, travel to foreign lands, and the onset of <term>Kshaya|wasting disease or tuberculosis</term>. || 4 ||

---

->**Gains of wealth and losses of wealth, happiness and sorrow from time to time;**<-
->**When the Moon is within Ketu, the gain of a woman also occurs. || 5 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Chandra|the Moon</term> (duration: 7 months <note>original: "मान-मासादि ७।०।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Ketu, there will be fluctuating fortunes—sometimes gaining wealth and sometimes losing it—intermittent periods of happiness and sorrow, and the acquisition of a wife or the company of women. || 5 ||

> ->**Arguments with kinsmen, fear of fire and thieves as well,**<-
> ->**And pain arises in the body when Mars is within Ketu. || 6 ||**<-

If in the <term>Dasha|major astrological period</term> of <term>Ketu|the South Node of the Moon</term>, the <term>Antardasha|sub-period</term> of <term>Mangal|Mars</term> (duration: 4 months and 27 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि ४।२७।०।०"</note>) occurs, there will be quarrels with brothers, fear of fire and thieves, and suffering within the body. || 6 ||

---

> ->**Fear of thieves, physical injury, and keeping company with wicked friends;**<-
> ->**Quarrels with enemies occur when Rahu is within Ketu. || 7 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Rahu|the North Node of the Moon</term> (duration: 12 months and 18 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि १२।१८।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Ketu, there will be fear of thieves, physical harm or injury <note>literally "breaking of the body"</note>, association with bad friends, and conflict with enemies. || 7 ||

---

> ->**Meetings with people honored by kings, and the arrival of wealth from the best of the twice-born;**<-
> ->**Gains of land and sons occur when Jupiter is within Ketu. || 8 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Brihaspati|Jupiter</term> (duration: 11 months and 6 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि ११।६।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Ketu, one will have unions or meetings with people who are respected by rulers, the arrival of wealth from <term>Brahmins|priests</term>, and the acquisition of land and the birth of a son. || 8 ||

---

> ->**Pain caused by wind and bile, and conflict with one’s own people;**<-
> ->**Traveling to foreign lands also occurs when Saturn is within Ketu. || 9 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Shani|Saturn</term> (duration: 13 months and 9 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि १३।९।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Ketu, one will suffer from ailments caused by <term>Vata|wind</term> and <term>Pitta|bile</term> <note>These are two of the three 'doshas' or humors in Ayurvedic medicine believed to govern health.</note>, quarrels with one's dear friends and relatives, and travel to foreign countries. || 9 ||

---

> ->**Reunion with friends and relatives, and conflict for the sake of land;**<-
> ->**Bodily pain will occur daily when Mercury is within Ketu. || 10 ||**<-

If the sub-period of <term>Budha|Mercury</term> (duration: 11 months and 27 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि ११।२७।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of Ketu, there will be meetings with friends and kinsmen, disputes regarding land, and constant physical pain. || 10 ||

<summary>This page details the specific astrological effects of the sub-periods of Mars, Rahu, Jupiter, Saturn, and Mercury during the major planetary cycle of Ketu. The predictions range from physical ailments and family conflicts to positive outcomes like the gain of land and wealth.</summary>
<keywords>Ketu, Mars, Rahu, Jupiter, Saturn, Mercury, Antardasha, Dasha, astrology, Vata, Pitta, Brahmin</keywords>

<header>Lagnachandrika (The Moonlight of the Ascendant)</header>

### Now, the results of Ketu within other planets

->**Exile, loss of relatives, loss of wealth, and the decline of sons;**<-
->**Sorrow indeed is obtained when Ketu enters within the Sun. || 1 ||**<-

If the <term>Antardasha|sub-period</term> of <term>Ketu|the South Node of the Moon</term> occurs during the <term>Dasha|major period</term> of the <term>Surya|Sun</term> (duration: 4 months and 6 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि ४।६।०।०"</note>), then the person may experience exile from their country, the death of relatives, the destruction of wealth and progeny, and general suffering. || 1 ||

---

->**Exile, loss of relatives, loss of wealth, and the decline of sons;**<-
->**Inauspiciousness prevails everywhere when Ketu enters within the Moon. || 2 ||**<-

If the sub-period of Ketu occurs during the major period of the <term>Chandra|Moon</term> (duration: 7 months <note>original: "मान-मासादि ७।०।०।०"</note>), there will be exile, the loss of relatives, sons, and wealth, and misfortune will follow the individual everywhere. || 2 ||

---

->**Great fear arises here from poison, weapons, fire, and thieves;**<-
->**A man always becomes a partaker of distress when Ketu enters within Mars. || 3 ||**<-

If Ketu is within the major period of <term>Mangal|Mars</term> (duration: 4 months and 27 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि ४।२७।०।०"</note>), there will be great fear from poisons, weapons, fire, and thieves, and the person will constantly suffer from various troubles. || 3 ||

### Now, the results of Ketu’s sub-period during the major period of Rahu

->**Death or its fear always comes from fever, fire, enemies, and weapons;**<-
->**Good fortune is never found when Ketu enters within Rahu. || 1 ||**<-

If the sub-period of Ketu occurs during the major period of <term>Rahu|the North Node of the Moon</term> (duration: 12 months and 18 days <note>original: "मान-मासादि १२।१८।०।०"</note>), there will be fear of death from fever, fire, enemies, and weapons, and auspicious results will not be obtained at any time. || 1 ||

---

->**Distress caused by sons and relatives, and deprived of one's own place;**<-
->**A man wanders about when Ketu enters within Jupiter. || 2 ||**<-

<note>The Hindi commentary for this verse is incomplete in the source text, but the Sanskrit verse indicates that during the sub-period of Ketu within the major period of <term>Guru|Jupiter</term>, the individual suffers anxiety regarding their children and kin, loses their home or position, and is forced to wander.</note>

<summary>This page details the generally malefic effects of Ketu's sub-periods when they occur during the major periods of the Sun, Moon, Mars, Rahu, and Jupiter, typically predicting loss, fear, and displacement.</summary>

## With the Vernacular Commentary

If a person is in the <term>Dasha|major planetary period</term> of <term>Brihaspati|Jupiter</term> and the <term>Antardasha|sub-period</term> of <term>Ketu|the South Node</term> (duration: 10 months and 6 days <note>original: "१०।६।०।०"</note>) occurs, they will become agitated or troubled by their sons and relatives, and may wander about having lost their sense of self. || 2 ||

> **Pain caused by blood-bile, and conflict with one's own kin;**
> **When Ketu enters within Saturn, a display of terrible sorrow occurs. || 3 ||**

If the sub-period of Ketu (duration: 1 year, 3 months, and 9 days <note>original: "१।३।९।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of <term>Shani|Saturn</term>, one must endure pain from <term>Raktapitta|a medical condition involving internal bleeding or excessive heat in the blood</term>, quarrels with family members, and the sight of horrific suffering. || 3 ||

> **Constantly overwhelmed by grief and sorrow, with the body joined to affliction;**
> **When Ketu enters within Mercury, this happens without a doubt. || 4 ||**

If the sub-period of Ketu (duration: 1 year, 1 month, and 27 days <note>original: "१।१।२७।०।०"</note>) occurs during the major period of <term>Budha|Mercury</term>, the individual will remain perpetually anxious with grief and sorrow, and the body will suffer from distress; there is no doubt about this. || 4 ||

This concludes the results of Ketu’s sub-periods among the planets. In this book, the results for the <term>Ashtottari Dasha|a 108-year planetary cycle system</term> and its sub-periods were shown first. One should understand the results for the <term>Vimshottari|the standard 120-year planetary cycle system</term> from these same predictions. The only difference is that in the Vimshottari system, the sequence is: Sun, Moon, Mars, Rahu, Jupiter, Saturn, Mercury, Ketu, and Venus. Wherever one needs to analyze or record the forecast, following this specific order will allow the results to be displayed clearly.

> **Starting from the birth-ascendant, add the elapsed years;**
> **Dividing by twelve, the auspicious and inauspicious results should be told via the planets. || 5 ||**

Add the years that have passed to the <term>Janma-Lagna|the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term>, and divide that sum by 12. Based on the remainder, one should state the good or bad results of the corresponding planets. || 5 ||

->**Now, the Monthly Periods**<-

> **Twenty days for the Sun, and fifty for the Moon;**
> **Twenty-seven for Mars (27), and fifty-seven (57) for the son of the Moon [Mercury]. || 1 ||**

<vocab>Brihaspati (Jupiter), Ketu (South Node), Antardasha (Sub-period), Shani (Saturn), Budha (Mercury), Ashtottari Dasha (108-year system), Vimshottari (120-year system), Janma-Lagna (Birth Ascendant), Masa-dasha (Monthly periods)</vocab>

<meta>This page continues the "Lagnachandrika" (Moon of the Ascendant), a classical Sanskrit text on astrology. While the previous page concluded the sub-periods of Ketu within the major planetary cycles, this page transitions into calculating shorter time-bound periods known as "Monthly Periods" (Masadasha) and "Daily Periods" (Dinadasha).</meta>

<page-num>132</page-num>

->**Lagnachandrika**<-

<gloss>33</gloss> There are thirty-three [days] for **Saturn**, and sixty-three <gloss>63</gloss> for **Jupiter**.
There are twenty 20 for **Rahu** <note>original: "Sainhikeya," a poetic name for Rahu, the son of Simhika</note>, and twenty for **Ketu** as well. || 2 ||
The wise should know there are seventy 70 [days] for **Venus** <note>original: "Bhriguputra," the son of Bhrigu</note> in these monthly periods.
One counts in order starting from the <term>Nam-Rashi|the zodiac sign associated with the first letter of a person's name</term> up to the <term>Sankranti|the moment the Sun enters a new zodiac sign</term>. || 3 ||

In the **Sun**, there are 20 days; in the **Moon**, 50 days; in **Mars**, 27 days; in **Mercury**, 57 days; in **Saturn**, 33 days; in **Jupiter**, 63 days; in **Rahu** and **Ketu**, 20 days; and in **Venus**, 70 days. One should count from the Name-Sign to the transition of the Sun; this is the period known by scholars. First, determine the period of the Sun, then the Moon, and so on, according to the method previously described. || 1–3 ||

## Now, the Daily Periods:

Take the number of the <term>Tithi|lunar day</term>, the day of the week, the <term>Nakshatra|lunar mansion</term>, and the number of letters in the person's name.
Divide this total by nine; the remainder is called the <term>Dinadasha|Daily Period</term>. || 1 ||

By adding the numerical values of the lunar day, the weekday, the lunar mansion, and the letters of the name, and then dividing by nine, one should declare the daily results based on the remainder, following the <term>Vimshottari|a 120-year planetary cycle system</term> sequence. || 1 ||

### Another Method

Double the number of months passed since <term>Chaitra|the first month of the Hindu calendar</term> and add the number of elapsed lunar days.
Divide this total by nine; the remainder is recorded as the daily result. || 1 ||
**Wealth**, **quarrel** with people, **joy**, the **thorn of time** <note>original: "Kala-kantaka," a period of great difficulty or misfortune</note>,
**Duty**, **penance**, and **victory**—these are the results in order, starting from Sunday. || 2 ||

Double the number of months passed since Chaitra and add the current lunar day, then divide by 9 to find the daily result. If the remainder is 1, it corresponds to Sunday—one obtains **wealth**; if the remainder is 2, it is Monday—there will be **quarrels** in the world; if 3, it is Tuesday—there will be **joy**; if 4, it is Wednesday—**Kala-kantaka** (misfortune); if 5... <note>The text cuts off here at the bottom of the page.</note>

<summary>This page defines the "Monthly Periods" (Masadasha) and "Daily Periods" (Dinadasha), providing specific mathematical formulas to calculate planetary influences based on a person's name, the current lunar date, and the calendar month.</summary>
<keywords>Lagnachandrika, Masadasha, Dinadasha, planetary periods, Tithi, Nakshatra, Chaitra, Vedic astrology, Kala-kantaka</keywords>
