<meta>This page continues the "Lagnachandrika," specifically concluding the calculation of "Daily Periods" (Dinadasha) and moving into the interpretation of planetary sub-periods, with a focus on "cruel" or malefic planetary combinations that signify physical danger or death.</meta>

<page-num>133</page-num>
<header>With English Translation of the Commentary</header>

...If the remainder is 5, it is Thursday—righteousness <note>original: "Dharma"</note> shall prevail. If the remainder is 6, it is Friday—one should perform penance <note>original: "Tapa"</note>. If the remainder is 7, it is Saturday—there shall be victory. If the remainder is 8, it is Rahu. If the remainder is 9, it is Ketu. These last two have a neutral or general result. || 1–2 ||

> If there is a sub-period of a malefic planet during the major period of a malefic planet, death occurs if they are in an inimical relationship; if they are in a friendly relationship, death remains uncertain. || 3 ||

In other words, if a sub-period <note>original: "Antardasha"</note> of a cruel planet <term>Krura Graha|A "cruel" or malefic planet, typically referring to the Sun, Mars, Saturn, Rahu, or Ketu</term> occurs during the major period <note>original: "Dasha"</note> of another cruel planet, then death occurs during a hostile alignment <note>original: "Shatruyoga"</note>. If there is a friendly alignment <note>original: "Mitrayoga"</note>, there is doubt regarding whether death will actually occur. || 3 ||

### Now, the results of Saturn's sub-period within the major period of Mars

> When the sub-period of Saturn occurs during the major period of Mars, even a person destined for a long life dies; what then to say of those with short lifespans? || 1 ||

If Saturn's sub-period occurs during the major period of Mars, even one who is a <term>Chiranjivi|A person destined for an exceptionally long life</term> may face death. What then is the need to speak of those with short lifespans? (That is to say, if even a long-lived person dies quickly under this influence, there is no doubt that a person with a short lifespan will perish). || 1 ||

### Now, the results of malefic planets within cruel planetary periods

> A malefic planet situated in a cruel sign, or in the sixth or eighth house, if aspected by Venus and the Sun, brings death during its own period of maturity. || 1 ||

If a malefic planet <note>original: "Paapa-graha"</note> is located in a zodiac sign belonging to a cruel planet, or is placed in the sixth or eighth house <note>original: "Nidhana," the house of death</note>, and is simultaneously seen <note>original: "Drishta," an astrological aspect</note> by Venus and the Sun, then that planet will bring about death during its own <term>Paka|The ripening or major period/sub-period of a planet's influence</term>. || 1 ||

> If an enemy of the Lord of the Ascendant is situated in the Ascendant and its sub-period arrives, it causes sudden death; this has been stated by Satyacharya. || 2 ||

If the enemy of the planet that rules the Ascendant <term>Lagna|The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</term> is positioned within the Ascendant itself, and the time for its sub-period arrives, it causes an unexpected death. This rule has been proclaimed by the Great Sage Satyacharya <note>A renowned historical authority in Indian horoscopic astrology</note>. || 2 ||

<vocab>Astrology, Major Period (Dasha), Sub-period (Antardasha), Cruel/Malefic Planet, Mars, Saturn, Death, Satyacharya, Malefic, Venus, Sun</vocab>

<header>Moon of the Ascendant</header>

### Now, the Cancellation of Afflictions during Planetary Periods

During a planetary period <term>Dasha|A specific time period in an individual's life ruled by a certain planet</term>, if the ruling planet is strong, or if it is aspected by benefic planets, or if a benefic planet is situated in the divisional chart of a "Great Friend" <term>Adhimitra-varga|A specific calculation in Vedic astrology where planets are ranked by their temporary and permanent friendliness to one another</term>, then the cancellation of afflictions <note>original: "Arishta-bhanga"</note> occurs during that period. || 1 ||

If, during the major period of the primary lord, the sub-period of Jupiter <note>original: "Vachaspati"</note> occurs, and if that period is determined to be strong and auspicious, then it should be known that the cancellation of afflictions takes place. || 2 ||

If a benefic planet <note>original: "Shubha-graha"</note> emerges victorious in a planetary conjunction <note>original: "Graha-yoga" or "Graha-yuddha"</note> and is situated in its sign of exaltation <term>Uchcha|The zodiac sign where a planet's influence is at its highest strength</term> or other favorable positions, it will not allow suffering to occur during its period. || 3 ||

->Thus ends the third chapter. || 3 ||<-

---

# Now, the Fourth Chapter || 4 ||

### The Conjunction of the Sun with Other Planets

One whose birth chart contains a conjunction of the Sun and the Moon <note>original: "Surya-chandra-samagama"</note> will be dominated by women, prone to deceitful behavior <note>original: "kuta-dharma"</note>, and lacking in proper etiquette. However, they will also be compassionate, courageous, and fickle-minded <term>Laghucheta|Literally 'small-hearted'; a person who is easily agitated, restless, or lacks emotional depth</term>. || 1 ||

<vocab>Period (Dasha), Affliction (Arishta), Planet (Graha), Jupiter (Brihaspati), Sun-Moon Conjunction (Surya-chandra-samagama), Deceitful nature (Kutadharma), Fickle-minded (Laghucheta)</vocab>

<summary>This page outlines how strong benefic planets or the influence of Jupiter can cancel out the "Arishta" (ill-omens or physical danger) of a planetary period, and begins describing the character traits of individuals born under specific planetary conjunctions, starting with the Sun and Moon.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the astrological analysis of planetary conjunctions from page 137. It concludes the section on Mercury (Budha) conjunctions and proceeds to the combinations involving Jupiter (Guru/Brihaspati) and Venus (Shukra/Bhṛgu) with other planets.</meta>

<page-num>138</page-num>
<header>Lagna Chandrika</header>

If there is a conjunction of **Mercury and Venus**, the person becomes wealthy, an eloquent speaker, a knower of the <term>Vedas|The ancient and most sacred scriptures of Hinduism</term>, one who takes interest in music and humor, a strategist <note>original: "nitijna," one who knows ethics, politics, or wise conduct</note>, and a thorough expert in the <term>Shilpa Shastra|The traditional Indian science of arts, crafts, and architecture</term>. || 2 ||

> ->One who is in debt, a wanderer, without resources, and very cruel;<-
> ->yet speaking auspicious words and skilled in poetry during the **conjunction of Mercury and Saturn**.<- || 3 ||

If there is a conjunction of **Mercury and Saturn** <note>original: "Budha-Manda"</note>, the person is in debt, a traveler, helpless, extremely harsh, yet a speaker of pleasant words and an expert in the art of poetry. || 3 ||

# Conjunctions of Two Planets with Jupiter

> ->Steadfast in religion, a knower of logic, and one who lives by knowledge;<-
> ->possessing a divine wife and great wealth in the **conjunction of Jupiter and Venus**.<- || 1 ||

If there is a conjunction of **Jupiter and Venus** <note>original: "Guru-Bhargava"</note>, the person has a firm standing in religion, is a knower of evidence <term>Pramana|The philosophical study of the means of attaining accurate knowledge</term>, earns a livelihood through education, possesses an excellent wife, and has much wealth. || 1 ||

> ->Success in occupation, brave, famous, and the lord of a city;<-
> ->a leader of guilds, armies, and villages is the man born under the **conjunction of Jupiter and Saturn**.<- || 2 ||

A child born under the conjunction of **Jupiter and Saturn** is successful in their professional life, brave, famous, the master of a city, and is considered a leader of guilds <note>original: "shreni," referring to professional or merchant corporations</note>, the army, and the village. || 2 ||

# Conjunctions of Two Planets with Venus

> ->Expert in splitting wood and knowing the craft of salts and acids;<-
> ->a wrestler, a master of cattle, with weak eyesight in the **conjunction of Saturn and Venus**.<- || 1 ||

A child born in the conjunction of **Venus and Saturn** <note>original: "Shani-Sita"</note> is skilled in the labor of splitting wood, knows the art of manufacturing alkaline or acidic substances <note>original: "kshara-amla," referring to early chemical or metallurgical processes</note>, is a wrestler, and an owner of cattle and buffaloes. || 1 ||

# With Language Commentary

## Now, the Conjunctions of Three Planets

**In the conjunction of the Sun, Mars, and Mercury, a man is known as daring. He is cruel, shameless, and suffers on account of wealth, his wife, and his sons. || 1 ||**

<note>original: "Inu-Bhauma-Budhaiḥ"</note> When a man is born under the conjunction of the **Sun, Mars, and Mercury**, he becomes famous for his boldness, but he is also pitiless and lacks a sense of shame. He faces hardships regarding his finances, his spouse, and his children. || 1 ||

**If there is a conjunction of the Sun, Mars, and Jupiter, one becomes powerful and a speaker of truth. He becomes a minister to a king, a leader of people, and an expert in speech. || 2 ||**

<note>original: "Sūrya-Bhauma-Ijya"</note> If the **Sun, Mars, and Jupiter** are in conjunction, the individual is formidable and truthful. He serves as a royal minister or a high-ranking official and is highly eloquent in his communication. || 2 ||

**When the Sun, Mars, and Venus are in conjunction, one is handsome and devoted to worship. He is of noble birth, affectionate in the world, yet his mind is inclined toward worldly pleasures. || 3 ||**

<note>original: "Sūrya-Ara-Śukra"</note> If the **Sun, Mars, and Venus** are joined, the person is beautiful and prosperous, dedicated to religious service, comes from a good family, and is well-liked by the public, though his mind remains preoccupied with sensory enjoyments. || 3 ||

**If the Sun, Mars, and Saturn are in conjunction, one is foolish and devoid of wealth and cattle. Afflicted by disease and abandoned by his kin, he is distressed and prone to quarreling. || 4 ||**

<note>original: "Sūrya-Ara-Śani"</note> When the **Sun, Mars, and Saturn** are in conjunction, the person lacks intelligence and resources. He suffers from physical ailments, loses the support of his relatives, and lives a life of agitation and conflict. || 4 ||

**The conjunction of the Sun, Mercury, and Jupiter makes a man suffer from eye disease but also makes him very wealthy. He is an expert in the art of weaponry and crafts and serves as a scribe. || 5 ||**

<note>The Sanskrit text specifies "Sūrya-Saumya-Ijya" (Sun-Mercury-Jupiter), though the Hindi commentary below it mistakenly mentions Venus (Shukra) instead of Jupiter.</note> When the **Sun, Mercury, and Jupiter** are in conjunction, the person may have vision problems, yet he possesses great wealth. He is skilled in technical arts <term>Shilpa|Traditional arts and crafts</term>, understands the science of weapons, and works as a writer or professional scribe. || 5 ||

**In the conjunction of the Sun, Mercury, and Venus, one is surrounded by teachers and elders. Accused or disgraced, he wanders to different lands, and his mind is distressed because of women. || 6 ||**

<note>original: "Sūrya-Jña-Śukra"</note> Under the conjunction of the **Sun, Mercury, and Venus**, the individual remains in the company of respected elders. However, he may face public censure, travel far from home, and suffer mental anguish relating to women. || 6 ||

# Lagna Chandrika
## The Moonlight of the Ascendant

If there is a **conjunction of the Sun, Mercury, and Venus**, the individual is dedicated to the service of his elders and teachers. He is an excellent person who follows the path of righteousness, yet his mind is often troubled or distressed on account of his wife or women in general. || 6 ||

> <note>original: "Sūrya-Jña-Śanibhi-yoge"</note> **If the Sun, Mercury, and Saturn are in conjunction, a man becomes a person of bad character and is frequently defeated. He is abandoned by his kinsmen and becomes full of malice or hatred. || 7 ||**

When the **Sun, Mercury, and Saturn** join together in a single house, the person tends toward immoral behavior and faces many losses. His own relatives may turn their backs on him, leading him to develop a resentful nature. || 7 ||

> <note>original: "Sūryejya-Śukra-saṃyoge"</note> **If there is a conjunction of the Sun, Jupiter, and Venus, one becomes a minister to a king but remains poor. He has bad eyes, is cruel, yet is also a scholar who works for the benefit of others. || 8 ||**

Under the influence of the **Sun, Jupiter, and Venus**, the individual may reach a high political station, such as a royal advisor, but will struggle to accumulate personal wealth. He may suffer from eye afflictions and possess a harsh temperament, though he remains a learned man (<term>Paṇḍit</term> — a scholar or wise man) who performs tasks for others. || 8 ||

> <note>original: "Sūryejya-Śani-bhiyoge"</note> **If the Sun, Jupiter, and Saturn are in conjunction, the person possesses sons, friends, and a wife. He is fearless, remains devoted to the king, and yet is an object of hatred for his kinsmen. || 9 ||**

The combination of the **Sun, Jupiter, and Saturn** grants a person a full family life with children and companions. Such a person is brave and loyal to the sovereign or state, but paradoxically, he faces enmity from his own family circle. || 9 ||

> <note>original: "Sūrya-Śukrārki-saṃyoge"</note> **If there is a conjunction of the Sun, Venus, and Saturn, the man is devoid of arts and honor. He may suffer from leprosy, is anxious about conquering enemies, and becomes a person of bad character. || 10 ||**

When the **Sun, Venus, and Saturn** meet, the individual lacks appreciation for the arts and loses his social standing. He is prone to chronic skin conditions <note>The term "kuṣṭhī" historically referred to various skin ailments, not exclusively leprosy in the modern sense.</note> and is perpetually worried about his rivals, leading him toward a dishonest life. || 10 ||

> <note>original: "Candrāra-Budha-saṃyoge"</note> **If the Moon, Mars, and Mercury are in conjunction, one is devoid of proper conduct and is a sinner. He lacks a livelihood in this world and is without kinsmen. || 11 ||**

The alignment of the **Moon, Mars, and Mercury** results in a person who ignores social and moral codes. He struggles to find a stable way to earn a living and finds himself isolated from his family and community. || 11 ||

> <note>original: "Candra-Bhaumejya-saṃyoge"</note> **If there is a conjunction of the Moon, Mars, and Jupiter, one is fond of women and marked by wounds. He is handsome, favored by women, and possesses a face as beautiful as the moon. || 12 ||**

<note>This verse describes a person of great physical beauty whose life is heavily influenced by his interactions with women. Despite his attractiveness, he bears physical scars or marks on his body.</note>

# Lagna Chandrika
## The Moonlight of the Ascendant

If there is a **conjunction of the Moon, Mars, and Jupiter**, the individual is restless or fickle when it comes to women. He may be afflicted by wounds or scars on the body (<term>Vraṇa</term> — often referring to ulcers or surgical marks), but he is highly regarded by beautiful women and possesses a handsome face, bright like the moon. || 12 ||

> <note>original: "Candrāra-bhṛgu-saṃyoge"</note> **If the Moon, Mars, and Venus are in conjunction, one becomes the husband or son of a woman of poor character. He is always prone to wandering and is fearful of the cold. || 13 ||**

When the **Moon, Mars, and Venus** join in the same house, the person is associated with a woman of ill-temper or questionable conduct—either as her husband or her son. Such an individual is constantly on the move, possessing a restless nature that keeps him traveling, and he suffers from a particular sensitivity or fear regarding cold weather. || 13 ||

> <note>original: "Candrāra-śanibhi-yoge"</note> **If the Moon, Mars, and Saturn are in conjunction, the individual’s mother dies during his childhood. He becomes a petty man, hated by the public, and is known to be difficult or crooked. || 14 ||**

Under the influence of the **Moon, Mars, and Saturn**, the person faces the misfortune of losing his mother early in life. He tends to develop a mean-spirited or insignificant character (<term>Kshudra</term> — meaning petty or low) and earns the enmity of those around him. His personality is often described as "uneven" or "crooked," suggesting a complex or harsh temperament. || 14 ||

> <note>original: "Candra-jña-jīvaiḥ saṃyoge"</note> **If there is a conjunction of the Moon, Mercury, and Jupiter, the person is illustrious and wealthy. He is endowed with sons and friends, is an eloquent speaker, and is famous and renowned. || 15 ||**

If the **Moon, Mercury, and Jupiter** are united, the individual leads a life of great success and prosperity. He enjoys the blessings of family (sons) and a wide social circle. His gift of speech makes him an eloquent orator, and his name becomes well-known and honored throughout society. || 15 ||

> <note>original: "Candra-jña-bhārgava-yoge"</note> **If the Moon, Mercury, and Venus are in conjunction, the man is endowed with knowledge. He is envious, extremely greedy for wealth, and behaves in a base or low manner. || 16 ||**

When the **Moon, Mercury, and Venus** come together, the person is certainly learned or educated, yet this knowledge does not prevent a flawed character. He is prone to jealousy and an insatiable desire for money, often resorting to behaviors that are considered beneath his status or morally low. || 16 ||

> <note>original: "Candra-jña-śanibhi-yoge"</note> **If the Moon, Mercury, and Saturn are in conjunction, the man is wise and honored by kings. He is very tall, has a large body, and is an eloquent speaker. || 17 ||**

Under the influence of the **Moon, Mercury, and Saturn**, the individual is recognized for his wisdom and earns the respect of high authorities or "kings." Physically, he is notable for his height and substantial build, and he possesses a talent for persuasive and clear speech. || 17 ||

# Lagna Chandrika
## The Moonlight of the Ascendant

If there is a conjunction of the **Moon (<term>Chandra</term>)**, **Mercury (<term>Budha</term>)**, and **Saturn (<term>Shani</term>)**, the person becomes a scholar (<term>Pandit</term>), is honored by the king, grows to be very tall with a heavy or robust body, and is known for speaking with great cleverness and wit. || 17 ||

> <note>original: "Candrejya-śukra-saṃyoge"</note> **If the Moon, Jupiter, and Venus are in conjunction, the individual is the son of a virtuous woman and is a scholar. He is a saintly person, a master of all the arts, handsome, and endowed with great wealth and power. || 18 ||**

When the **Moon**, **Jupiter (<term>Brihaspati</term>)**, and **Venus (<term>Shukra</term>)** join together, the individual is born to a noble and faithful mother. Such a person is highly learned, lives a righteous or saintly life, and possesses knowledge of all the fine arts (<term>Kala</term>). He is physically attractive and enjoys a life of prosperity and influence. || 18 ||

> <note>original: "Candrejya-śanibhi-yoge"</note> **If the Moon, Jupiter, and Saturn are in conjunction, the individual is free from disease and devoted to his wife. He is a knower of the meaning of the scriptures, an expert in ethics and policy, and a protector of villages and cities. || 19 ||**

Under the influence of the **Moon**, **Jupiter**, and **Saturn**, the person enjoys good health and remains loyal to his spouse. He is well-versed in the interpretation of sacred texts (<term>Shastra-artha</term>) and the principles of governance (<term>Niti</term>). Consequently, he often holds a position of authority, acting as a guardian or administrator of towns and settlements. || 19 ||

> <note>original: "Candra-śukrārki-bhi-yoge"</note> **If the Moon, Venus, and Saturn are in conjunction, the individual is a scribe, a knower of the Vedas, born into a family of priests, and a reader of books. || 20 ||**

If the **Moon**, **Venus**, and **Saturn** meet in the birth chart, the individual often pursues a career in writing or clerical work (<term>Lipikartta</term>). Born into a lineage of traditional priests (<term>Purohit</term>), he is learned in the <term>Vedas</term> (ancient scriptures) and finds his calling in the study and recitation of sacred books. || 20 ||

> <note>original: "Bhaumajña-jīvaiḥ saṃyoge"</note> **If Mars, Mercury, and Jupiter are in conjunction, the individual is an excellent poet and the husband of a young woman. He is a philanthropist, possesses a sharp intellect, and is skilled in the Gandharva arts. || 21 ||**

When **Mars (<term>Mangal</term>)**, **Mercury**, and **Jupiter** join together, the person becomes a gifted poet and marries a beautiful, youthful woman. He is characterized by his desire to help others and his "sharp" or incisive intelligence. Furthermore, he is highly proficient in the <term>Gandharva Vidya</term>—the traditional arts of music, dance, and performance. || 21 ||

> <note>original: "Bhaumajña-bhṛgu-bhi-yoge"</note> **If Mars, Mercury, and Venus are in conjunction, the individual has deformed limbs and is restless. He is of low birth, yet always enthusiastic, satisfied (or arrogant), and a talkative person. || 22 ||**

If **Mars**, **Mercury**, and **Venus** are in conjunction, the individual may suffer from physical disabilities or "disturbed" limbs (<term>Vikalanga</term>). His nature is inherently fickle or unstable. Though he may come from a humble or unrefined family background, he remains perpetually energetic. He tends to be self-satisfied (sometimes bordering on prideful) and is known for being quite talkative or loud. || 22 ||

# Lagna Chandrika
## With Vernacular Commentary

->**If Mars, Mercury, and Saturn are in conjunction, the person resides in foreign lands, suffers from eye diseases, acts as a servant, has ailments of the mouth, and is overly fond of jesting. || 23 ||**<-

If there is a conjunction of **Mars (<term>Mangala</term>)**, **Mercury (<term>Budha</term>)**, and **Saturn (<term>Shani</term>)**, the individual will live in a foreign land (<term>Pravasi</term>), suffer from diseases of the eyes, work as a servant, have diseases affecting the face or mouth, and will be a seeker of humor or laughter. || 23 ||

---

->**If Mars, Jupiter, and Venus are in conjunction, the person is united with a divine woman, is happy, brings joy to everyone in the world, and becomes a favorite of the king. || 24 ||**<-

When the conjunction of **Mars**, **Jupiter (<term>Brihaspati</term>)**, and **Venus (<term>Shukra</term>)** occurs, the individual is joined with a beautiful or "divine" woman and lives happily. Such a person brings joy to everyone in the world and is well-liked by the ruler or government. || 24 ||

---

->**If Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn are in conjunction, the person has a wounded body, is approved by the king, yet follows lowly conduct, is pitiless, and is censured by his friends. || 25 ||**<-

If there is a conjunction of **Mars (<term>Bhauma</term>)**, **Jupiter (<term>Jiva</term>)**, and **Saturn (<term>Arki</term>)**, the person will have scars or wounds on their limbs (<term>Kshata-anga</term>). While they may be respected by the king, they tend toward base or low behavior (<term>Nicha-achara</term>), lack compassion (<term>Nirghrina</term>), and are criticized by their own circle of friends. || 25 ||

---

->**If Mars, Venus, and Saturn are in conjunction, the person becomes the husband of an ill-behaved woman and the son of such a woman; he is prone to wandering in foreign lands and is always miserable. || 26 ||**<-

When **Mars**, **Venus**, and **Saturn** join together, the individual becomes the husband of a woman with a difficult or wicked temperament and is likewise the son of a woman with a similar nature. He is inclined to live abroad and remains constantly unhappy. || 26 ||

---

->**If Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus are in conjunction, the person has a beautiful body, is honored by the king, destroys his enemies, possesses long-lasting fame, and is a speaker of truth. || 27 ||**<-

If there is a conjunction of **Mercury**, **Jupiter**, and **Venus**, the person possesses a handsome physique, is worshiped or honored by the king, triumphs over his enemies, attains great and lasting fame, and is a truthful person. || 27 ||

---

->**If Mercury, Jupiter, and Saturn are in conjunction, the person has a good wife, enjoys many pleasures, is endowed with wealth and power, and is generally characterized by happiness and patience. || 28 ||**<-

<note>The following list provides the various Sanskrit synonyms used in the text for the planets and astrological terms: **Kuja/Mangala/Bhauma** (Mars), **Jna/Budha** (Mercury), **Shani/Arki** (Saturn), **Ijya/Jiva/Brihaspati** (Jupiter), **Bhrigu/Shukra** (Venus), **Jyotisha** (Astrology), **Yoga** (Planetary combination).</note>

Having previously discussed combinations of two and three planets, it concludes the three-planet section (Tri-graha Yoga) and begins the analysis of four-planet groupings (Chatur-graha Yoga). The translation ensures that the technical Sanskrit terms for planets are identified while keeping the main text accessible.</meta>

# Lagna Chandrika
## With Vernacular Commentary

->**If there is a conjunction of Mercury, Jupiter, and Saturn, the individual will have a beautiful wife, experience many worldly pleasures, be endowed with wealth and glory, and be particularly characterized by happiness and patience. || 28 ||**<-

If **Mercury** (<term>Budha</term>), **Jupiter** (<term>Guru</term>), and **Saturn** (<term>Shani</term>) are joined together in a single house, the person will have a beautiful wife, enjoy many comforts, possess wealth and magnificence, and be especially blessed with a peaceful, patient disposition. || 28 ||

---

> **If Mercury, Venus, and Saturn are in conjunction, the person is talkative, wanders after others' wives, is unskilled in the arts, lacks good company, and remains in his own country. || 29 ||**

If there is a conjunction of **Mercury**, **Venus** (<term>Shukra</term>), and **Saturn**, the person will be talkative or boastful, prone to adultery <note>original: "para-dara-ga", literally 'one who goes to another's wife'</note>, lack the company of virtuous people (<term>satsanga</term>), be ignorant of various arts or skills, and will always reside in his own native land rather than traveling. || 29 ||

---

> **If Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn are in conjunction, the person becomes a famous king; even if born in a lowly family, they are virtuous and the best among rulers. || 30 ||**

When **Jupiter** (<term>Brihaspati</term>), **Venus**, and **Saturn** are together, the individual becomes a renowned ruler. Even if they are born into a humble or low-status family (<note>original: "nicha-vanshe-api", meaning 'even in a low lineage'</note>), they will possess excellent character and be considered a superior king. || 30 ||

---

> **Generally, if the Moon is joined with malefic planets, it causes the destruction of the mother; if the Sun is so joined, the father. With benefic planets, one should predict auspicious results; with mixed planets, the results are mixed. || 31 ||**

As a general rule, the **Moon** (<term>Chandra</term>) combined with "cruel" or malefic planets (<term>papa-graha</term>) <note>Such as Mars or Saturn</note> brings harm or death to the mother. If the **Sun** (<term>Surya</term>) is similarly joined, it brings harm to the father. If joined with benefic planets, the results are good, and if the planets are of a mixed nature, the results will be a blend of good and bad. || 31 ||

---

> **Three benefic planets joined together make a person happy. Three malefic planets make a person miserable, ill-behaved, and censured. || 32 ||**

If three benefic planets are situated together, they grant the person a happy life. Conversely, three malefic planets together make a person sorrowful, rude or ill-mannered, and subject to public criticism or reproach. || 32 ||

---

### Now, the Conjunctions of Four Planets

> **In the conjunction of the Sun, Moon, Mercury, and Mars, one becomes a scribe, a thief, talkative, an orator, skilled in illusion, and a physician. || 1 ||**

<note>The Sanskrit text "Arke-Indu-ja-Saumyanam" literally lists names associated with the Sun, Moon, and Mercury; however, in the context of the four-planet series in traditional Jyotisha, this refers to the combination of the **Sun**, **Moon**, **Mercury**, and **Mars**.</note>

<summary>This page concludes the astrological effects of three-planet conjunctions, emphasizing how combinations like Jupiter-Venus-Saturn can elevate a person to kingship, and introduces the section on four-planet conjunctions beginning with the Sun-Moon-Mercury-Mars grouping.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the classification of planetary conjunctions from the Lagna Chandrika. It moves into the "Chatur-graha Yoga" (four-planet combinations), providing predictions for various groupings starting with the Sun and Moon combined with two other planets.</meta>

<page-num>145</page-num>
# Lagna Chandrika
## With Vernacular Commentary

If there is a conjunction of the **Sun**, **Moon**, **Mars**, and **Mercury**, the individual will have the temperament of a child, be a writer, a thief, outspoken <note>original: "muñhaphaṭ", meaning blunt or one who speaks without filter</note>, an eloquent speaker, a deceiver <note>original: "mayavi", suggesting one who uses illusions or tricks</note>, and will be knowledgeable in the medical arts. || 1 ||

->**If the Sun, Moon, Mars, and Jupiter are in conjunction, the person is skillful, wealthy, radiant, free from sorrow, and well-versed in ethics and policy. || 2 ||**<-

If the **Sun** (<term>Surya</term>), **Moon** (<term>Chandra</term>), **Mars** (<term>Kuja</term>), and **Jupiter** (<term>Jiva</term>) are joined together, the person will be highly capable, prosperous, majestic, free from grief, and possess deep knowledge of <term>Niti</term> (political and moral ethics). || 2 ||

---

->**If the Sun, Moon, Mars, and Venus are in conjunction, the person collects knowledge and wealth, is happy, possesses sons and a wife, and earns a living through speech. || 3 ||**<-

If the **Sun**, **Moon**, **Mars**, and **Venus** (<term>Shukra</term>) are in conjunction, the individual will be a gatherer of both learning and riches. They will be happy, blessed with children and a spouse, and will pursue a profession involving their voice <note>original: "vag-vritti", such as a teacher, singer, or orator</note>. || 3 ||

---

->**If the Sun, Moon, Mars, and Saturn are in conjunction, the person is a fool, poor, short of stature, has an irregular body, and lives by begging. || 4 ||**<-

If the **Sun**, **Moon**, **Mars**, and **Saturn** (<term>Manda</term>) are joined, the person will lack wisdom, be impoverished, have a short height, possess an asymmetrical or deformed body, and will sustain themselves through alms. || 4 ||

---

->**If the Sun, Moon, Mercury, and Jupiter are in conjunction, the person is a craftsman, wealthy, a goldsmith, has sunken eyes, and is free from disease. || 5 ||**<-

If the **Sun**, **Moon**, **Mercury** (<term>Budha</term>), and **Jupiter** are in conjunction, the individual will be an artisan <note>original: "shilpakara", a craftsman or architect</note>, wealthy, a dealer in gold or a jeweler, have deep-set eyes, and will remain healthy throughout life. || 5 ||

---

->**If the Sun, Moon, Mercury, and Venus are in conjunction, the person is handsome, fortunate, short, respected by the king, eloquent, and agitated. || 6 ||**<-

If the **Sun**, **Moon**, **Mercury**, and **Venus** are joined, the person will be beautiful and prosperous, short in stature, honored by the government or royalty, a clever speaker, and yet prone to being distressed or restless <note>original: "vikala", meaning agitated or lacking a limb/part, but usually used here for mental state</note>. || 6 ||

# Lagna Chandrika

If there is a conjunction of the **Sun**, **Moon**, **Mercury**, and **Saturn**, the individual will subsist on food obtained by begging. They will live separated from their mother and father, possess impaired or defective eyes, and be destitute. || 7 ||
<note>The Sanskrit "vikalaksha" suggests a physical impairment of the eyes or perhaps a squint.</note>

If the **Sun**, **Moon**, **Jupiter**, and **Venus** are in coordination, the individual will be honored by the king. They will be the master of reservoirs <note>original: "jalashaya", meaning ponds or lakes</note>, forests, and deer. Such a person is skillful and happy. || 8 ||

If the **Sun**, **Moon**, **Jupiter**, and **Saturn** form a conjunction, the individual will be highly respected and beloved by women. They will possess great wealth and many sons, have a sharp or intense temperament, and have symmetrical or even eyes. || 9 ||

If there is a conjunction of the **Sun**, **Moon**, **Venus**, and **Saturn**, the individual will be physically very weak. They will behave in a feminine manner <note>original: "vanita-sadrisha-acharo", meaning conduct resembling a woman's</note>, be fearful or timid, yet they will be a leader <note>original: "agresara", one who walks in front or leads</note>. || 10 ||

If the **Sun**, **Mars**, **Mercury**, and **Jupiter** are in conjunction, the individual will be a weaver or thread-worker <note>original: "sutrakara", one who works with thread</note>. They will be an adulterer, a brave warrior, and yet remain sorrowful; they will be a "wheel-bearer" <note>original: "chakradhara", which could signify a potter, a disc-bearing soldier, or a person of authority</note>. || 11 ||

If the **Sun**, **Moon**, **Mars**, and **Venus** are in conjunction, the individual will be an adulterer, shameless, a wicked person, and a thief. They will possess uneven or deformed limbs. || 12 ||

If there is a conjunction of the **Sun**, **Moon**, **Mars**, and **Venus**, the individual will be one who keeps another's wife <note>an adulterer</note>, shameless, a wicked person, a thief, and will possess an asymmetrical or uneven body. || 12 ||

-> Through the union of the Sun, Mars, Mercury, and Saturn, one becomes a warrior and a poet. <-
-> They become a minister, a king, intense in temperament, and one of low conduct.  <-

If the **Sun**, **Mars**, **Mercury**, and **Saturn** are in conjunction, the individual will be a warrior, a poet, a minister, a general, intense <note>original: "teekshna", meaning sharp or fierce</note>, and possess low or degraded conduct. || 13 ||

-> Through the union of the Sun, Mars, Jupiter, and Venus, the person is venerable and wealthy. <-
-> They are fortunate, honored by the king, famous, and a follower of ethics. <-

If the **Sun**, **Mars**, **Jupiter**, and **Venus** are in conjunction, the individual will be venerable, wealthy, beautiful, prosperous, honored by the king, famous, and knowledgeable in <term>Niti</term> <note>Niti refers to political ethics, statecraft, or moral conduct</note>. || 14 ||

-> Through the union of the Sun, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn, one becomes a leader of the people. <-
-> Such a man is passionate, honored by the king, and one whose goals are attained. <-

If the **Sun**, **Mars**, **Jupiter**, and **Saturn** are in conjunction, the individual will be a leader of the <term>Ganas</term> <note>original: "Gananayaka"; a leader of a group, community, or guild</note>, a leader among many men, passionate or intoxicated <note>original: "unmadi", which can range from divinely inspired to manic</note>, honored by the king, and one who fulfills his purposes. || 15 ||

-> Through the union of the Sun, Mars, Venus, and Saturn, a man is born. <-
-> He is a hater of the world, has uniform eyes, low conduct, and a dull appearance. <-

If the **Sun**, **Mars**, **Venus**, and **Saturn** are in conjunction, the individual will be an enemy to the people, possess uniform or "even" eyes <note>original: "samanu", perhaps implying a fixed or blank stare</note>, engage in low conduct, and have a dull or heavy physical form. || 16 ||

-> Through the union of the Sun, Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus, a man of great intelligence is born. <-
-> He is wealthy, happy, successful in his aims, and bold. <-

If the **Sun**, **Mercury**, **Jupiter**, and **Venus** are in conjunction, the individual will be highly intelligent, remain wealthy and happy, achieve his objectives, and be <term>Pragalbha</term> <note>original: "Pragalbha" means bold, confident, or mature in speech</note>. || 17 ||

->**Through the conjunction of the Sun, Mercury, Jupiter, and Saturn, a man is born.**<-
->**He possesses brothers, is quarrelsome and proud, but acts without virility and is lazy. || 18 ||**<-

If the **Sun**, **Mercury**, **Jupiter**, and **Saturn** are located in a single sign, the individual will have brothers but will be prone to conflict, possess great pride, behave in a manner lacking masculine vigor <note>original: "klībācārī", behaving like a eunuch</note>, and will be devoid of industriousness or effort. || 18 ||

->**Through the union of the Sun, Mercury, Venus, and Saturn, one is joined with friends and is pure.**<-
->**Such a man is talkative, beautiful, wise, and becomes happy. || 19 ||**<-

->**The man becomes a poet, a leader of artisans, and is loved by the king. || 20 ||**<-

If there is a union of the **Moon**, **Mars**, **Mercury**, and **Jupiter**, the person becomes highly skilled in the <term>Shastras</term> <note>traditional Sanskrit scriptures and sciences</note>, a favorite of the king's grace, and exceptionally intelligent. || 21 ||

->**The man becomes sleepy, quarrelsome, base, and a hater of his own relatives. || 22 ||**<-

If there is a union of the **Moon**, **Mars**, **Mercury**, and **Venus**, the individual becomes the husband of an unchaste woman <note>the Sanskrit "bandhaki" usually refers to a woman of loose morals; the Hindi commentary here uses "bandhya," meaning barren</note>, is prone to excessive sleep, is quarrelsome, low-minded, and feels hatred toward his own kinsmen. || 22 ||

**Through the union of the Moon, Mars, Mercury, and Saturn, one is born into a heroic lineage.**
**Such a person is joined by sons, friends, and a wife, and possesses two mothers and two fathers. || 23 ||**

If there is a conjunction of the **Moon**, **Mars**, **Venus**, and **Saturn**, the individual is born into a family of brave warriors, is blessed with children, friends, and a spouse, and is characterized as having two mothers and two fathers <note>original: "dvimātṛpitṛko"; this traditionally refers to having both biological and adoptive parents, or being raised by a step-mother or maternal aunt.</note>. || 23 ||
<note>Translator's note: The Sanskrit verse specifies Mercury (Jna), but the Hindi commentary provided in the OCR mentions Venus (Shukra). Based on the Sanskrit, the combination is Moon-Mars-Mercury-Saturn.</note>

**Through the union of the Moon, Mars, Jupiter, and Venus, a man becomes daring.**
**He may have a physical disability, yet he is wealthy, has sons, is proud, and is also of a wise nature. || 24 ||**

If there is a conjunction of the **Moon**, **Mars**, **Jupiter**, and **Venus**, the person will be headstrong or daring, may possess a physical deformity <note>original: "vikalāṅgo", meaning missing or impaired limbs</note>, but will be wealthy, possess sons, be full of self-respect, and have the disposition of a scholar. || 24 ||

**Through the conjunction of the Moon, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn, one is deaf and impoverished.**
**He may suffer from mental instability, yet possesses steady speech, is heroic, and becomes a wise man. || 25 ||**

If the **Moon**, **Mars**, **Jupiter**, and **Saturn** meet, the individual will be hard of hearing, destitute, subject to bouts of mania or insanity <note>original: "sonmādaḥ"</note>, but will be firm in his word, a brave warrior, and a learned person. || 25 ||

**Through the meeting of the Moon, Mars, Venus, and Saturn, one becomes the husband of an unchaste woman.**
**Such a man is full of anxiety, has eyes like a serpent, and is bold. || 26 ||**

If there is a union of the **Moon**, **Mars**, **Venus**, and **Saturn**, the individual will be the husband of a disloyal woman, will be filled with agitation, possess eyes resembling those of a snake <note>original: "sarpatulyākṣaḥ", likely referring to a piercing or unusual gaze</note>, and will be a hypocrite <note>original Sanskrit "pragalbha" usually means mature or bold, but the Hindi commentary interprets it as "pākhaṇḍī" or hypocritical.</note>. || 26 ||

**Through the meeting of the Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus, one is fortunate and wealthy.**
**He has two mothers and fathers, is wise, and lives without enemies. || 27 ||**

If the **Moon**, **Venus**, **Mercury**, and **Jupiter** are in conjunction, the individual will be handsome, prosperous, wealthy, will have two mothers and two fathers, will be a scholar, and will have no enemies. || 27 ||

**Through the union of the Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, and Saturn, one is dear to his kinsmen and a poet.**
**He is majestic, a minister to the king, and a man possessed of fame and righteousness. || 28 ||**

<note>technical terms:
- **Lagna Chandrika**: The title of this astrological treatise, meaning "Moonlight of the Ascendant."
- **Jyotish**: The traditional Indian system of astrology.
- **Yoga**: A specific planetary combination or "union" that produces a particular result.
- **Graha**: Celestial bodies or "planets" used in Indian astrology.
</note>

<header>With the Vernacular Commentary</header>

## ->With the Vernacular Commentary<-

If there is a meeting of **Mars, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn**, the person is fond of bold deeds <note>original: "sāhasa-priyaḥ"; literally, one who loves daring or impulsive acts</note>, wealthy, radiant, fickle toward women, and becomes a rogue. || 34 ||
*If Mars, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn are in conjunction, the person is stubborn, wealthy, illustrious, restless among women, and cunning. || 34 ||*

Through the union of **Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn**, a man becomes overcome by desire, one who commands servants, intelligent, sharp-natured, and devoted to the scriptures <note term="shāstra">sacred or technical treatises of Indian philosophy and science</note>. || 35 ||
*If there is a union of Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn, the person is lustful, causes others to serve him, is wise, of a sharp temperament, and is engaged in the study of the scriptures. || 35 ||*

### ->Now, the Conjunctions of Five Planets<-

A man born under the five planets starting from the **Sun and ending with Jupiter** <note>This refers to the Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, and Jupiter</note> is very deceitful, unhappy, and suffers from separation from his wife. || 1 ||
*If the Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, and Jupiter are in conjunction, the person is very illusory, sorrowful, and separated from his wife. || 1 ||*

One becomes devoid of truth, lacking in kinsmen, a worker of others' deeds, and a friend of the impotent <note>original: "klība"; often referring to eunuchs or those outside traditional gender roles</note> through the union of the **Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, and Venus**. || 2 ||
*If these planets—Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, and Venus—are present, the person is untruthful, without relatives, does the work of others, and is a companion to eunuchs. || 2 ||*

Through the union of the **Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, and Saturn**, one has a short life, is without a wife, is unhappy, lacks a son, and also faces the fate of imprisonment. || 3 ||
*If the Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, and Saturn are in one place, the person will have a short lifespan, be without a wife, sorrowful, childless, and a participant in bondage <note>referring to being jailed or restrained</note>. || 3 ||*

> A man becomes tall, covered in hair, enthusiastic about death, and devoid of wealth, sons, and happiness when the **Sun, Moon, Mercury, Venus, and Saturn** are joined. || 9 ||

*If the Sun, Mars, Mercury, Venus, and Saturn are joined, the person will constantly suffer from ailments and enemies, be ousted from his home or status, suffer from hunger, and remain eternally agitated. || 13 ||*

<header>Moon-Sign Moonlight (Lagna Chandrika)</header>

> A man becomes wise, thoughtful regarding metallurgy, mechanics, and alchemy, and famous when the **Sun, Mars, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn** are joined. || 14 ||

*If the Sun, Mars, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn are situated together in one place, the person is a scholar, thoughtful regarding metals, machinery <note>original: "dhatu-yantra"</note>, and alchemy <term>Rasayana</term> <note>the traditional Indian science of alchemy and life-extension</note>, and becomes a famous individual. || 14 ||*

> One is dear to friends, an expert in the scriptures, religious, respected by teachers, and compassionate through the union of the **Sun, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn**. || 15 ||

*If the Sun, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn are in conjunction, the person is loved by his friends, a scholar of the scriptures, religious, an advisor to his elders or teachers <note>original: "guru-sammatah"</note>, and kind-hearted. || 15 ||*

> A man is virtuous, free from sin, endowed with wealth, knowledge, and happiness, and possesses many friends when the **Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus** are together. || 16 ||

*One whose Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus are in one place is a saintly person <term>Sadhu</term>, free of sin, filled with wealth, education, and joy, and has a great many friends. || 16 ||*

> A man becomes a cook for others, destitute, unclean, and afflicted by eye disease through the union of the **Moon, Mars, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn**. || 17 ||

*If the Moon, Mars, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn are in conjunction, the person becomes a cook for others <note>implying a status of servitude or dependence</note>, poor, dirty, and suffers from a disease of darkness <term>Timira</term> <note>a traditional Ayurvedic term for various eye diseases, including cataracts or night blindness</note>. || 17 ||*

> Having many friends and many enemies, ill-behaved, an oppressor of others, and proud is the man born under the union of the **Moon, Mars, Mercury, Venus, and Saturn**. || 18 ||

*If the Moon, Mars, Mercury, Venus, and Saturn are in one place, the person has both many friends and many enemies, a wicked nature, causes pain to others, and is arrogant. || 18 ||*

> A royal minister, equal to a king, respected by the people, and a leader of the group <term>Ganadhipa</term> is the man born under the union of the **Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and the Sun**. || 19 ||

<summary>This page lists specific five-planet astrological conjunctions and their predicted effects on a person's life, ranging from high-status roles like royal ministers to lower-status positions such as servants or those afflicted with chronic illness.</summary>

<header>Translation with Commentary</header>

If the **Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn** are situated in one place, the person becomes a minister to a king, is honorable like a king, is worshipped by the public, and becomes a master over many men. || 19 ||

->Without sorrow, irritable, destitute, insane, a royal favorite,<-
->And overcome by sleep through Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn. || 20 ||<-

If there is a union of **Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn**, the person is free from grief <note>original: "ashoka"</note>, possesses a <term>Tamasic</term> <note>a quality associated with darkness, anger, or inertia</note> or angry temperament, is poor, prone to madness, a favorite of the king, and remains under the influence of sleep. || 20 ||

### Now, the Combinations of Six Planets

->Endowed with knowledge, virtue, and wealth, talkative and fortunate;<-
->Profit comes from six planets, from the Sun through to Venus. || 1 ||<-

If the **Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus** are together in one place, the child is endowed with knowledge and wealth, is very talkative, and is highly fortunate. || 1 ||

->Devoted to others' work, a donor, pure-souled, and of restless form;<-
->With six planets excluding Venus, the person delights in solitude. || 2 ||<-

Excluding Venus, if the **Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, and Saturn**—these six planets—are united, the person is devoted to the work of others, is charitable, has a pure soul, a restless appearance, and enjoys residing in solitary places. || 2 ||

->Doubtful, handsome, proud, famous, a crusher of enemies in battle;<-
->Without Jupiter, with six planets, the person delights in forests. || 3 ||<-

If the **Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Venus, and Saturn**—these six planets—are in one place, the person has a doubtful mind, is handsome, wealthy, proud, famous, a crusher of enemies in battle <note>original: "ari-mardakah"</note>, and is a lover of wandering in forests and wilderness. || 3 ||

--- Page 160 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific astrological yogas (combinations) for six planets (Shad-Graha) from the "Lagna Chandrika." The previous page established the results for conjunctions involving the Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, and Jupiter, as well as clusters excluding Venus.</meta>

<page-num>156</page-num>
<header>Lagna Chandrika (Moon of the Ascendant)</header>

->**A lover of wealth, enthusiastic for battle, a backbiter, possessing anger and greed;**<-
->**Through the Sun, Moon, Mars, Venus, Jupiter, and Saturn, the man becomes fortunate. || 4 ||**<-

If the **Sun, Moon, Mars, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn** <note>original Sanskrit uses 'Arka' for Sun, 'Indu' for Moon, 'Bhauma' for Mars, 'Ijya' for Jupiter, and 'Manda' for Saturn</note> are together in one place, the person is a lover of wealth, enthusiastic for war, a slanderer, prone to anger and greed, yet also handsome and possessing great opulence and power. || 4 ||

---

->**Bereft of a wife, without wealth, a royal minister, and endowed with forgiveness;**<-
->**Through the Sun, Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn, the man becomes fortunate. || 5 ||**<-

If the **Sun, Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn** are in one place, the person is deprived of a wife and material wealth, yet becomes a minister to a king, possesses a forgiving nature, and is handsome. || 5 ||

---

->**Destitute of wealth, wife, and sons, a traveler to holy sites, and a forest-dweller;**<-
->**In the union of the Sun, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn, such a man is born. || 6 ||**<-

If the **Sun, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn** are in one place, the person is deprived of wealth, a wife, and sons; they become a pilgrim to holy places <note>original: "tirthayatri"</note> and a resident of the forest <note>implying an ascetic or solitary life</note>. || 6 ||

---

->**Wealthy, having sons, pure, a minister, having many wives, and a favorite of the king;**<-
->**Without the Sun, through these six planets, a powerful man is born. || 7 ||**<-

If the **Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn**—these six planets excluding the Sun—are together in one place, the person becomes wealthy, has many sons, is pure of heart, serves as a minister, has many wives, is a favorite of the king, and is very powerful and majestic. || 7 ||

---

->**Generally poor and foolish with six or five planets;**<-
->**This result is proclaimed due to their mutual aspect. || 8 ||**<-

If six or five planets are together in one place, the <term>Jataka</term> <note>the subject whose birth chart is being read</note> is often poor and foolish; however, this specific negative result is said to occur only when there is a mutual aspect <note>original: "drishti," the "glance" or influence one planet exerts on another from a distance</note> between them. || 8 ||
